# Log

Append-only chronological record of what happened in the wiki and when — ingests,
queries, lint passes. One line per event, newest at the bottom.

Each entry uses a greppable prefix so `grep "^## \[" wiki/log.md | tail -5` returns
the last 5 events.

Format (each real entry is an `## [date] type | detail` heading):

- `[date] ingest | <Source title> — touched: <N pages>`
- `[date] query  | <question in a few words>`
- `[date] lint   | <count> issues found`

## [2026-06-19] research | EM×agents + substrate + people watch (lookback 14d, headless scheduled run) — filed: 2 raw sources (both Rico Fritzsche, ricofritzsche.me, 2026-06-17 — public full-text Ghost posts, verbatim to raw/articles/). These are the RPU/Reactor terminology overhaul FLAGGED as out-of-window backlog on 06-18, now PUBLISHED IN-WINDOW and public: (1) raw/articles/rico-fritzsche-request-processing-units-and-reactors — retires "Feature Slice"; defines the new vocabulary (RPU = atomic self-contained processor for one Command/Query domain capability; Reactor = optional lightweight coordinator of several RPUs/Providers for one Interaction, NOT an event handler/saga/projection; Interaction = user-facing process; Use Case = stakeholder-level grouping of Interactions; Delivery Mechanism = thin HTTP/CLI translation; Providers = injected infrastructure; Event Store = application state); RPUs decoupled from transport (no longer "contain everything incl. HTTP"); duplication-over-DRY; recommended folder structure (rpunits/ reactors/ http/ events/ providers/). (2) raw/articles/rico-fritzsche-domain-defined-by-capabilities-not-object-models — the deeper argument: layered architectures = "distributed technical ownership" (one capability fragmented across controller/service/repo/entity/mapper); even VSA often leaves shared internal ownership under the slice; the domain capability should be THE ownership boundary; domain behaves as a deterministic functional core over immutable facts; additive evolution; "business rules live in the domain, not in aggregates — that's the problem with DDD." Both update [[autonomous-domain-capabilities]] / [[rico-fritzsche]] / [[business-capabilities]] / [[vertical-slice-architecture]], ready to ingest. Topic-1 (tight EM×agents) quiet — only the already-filed Dymitruk "future-proof / users-or-processors" X post + generic LLM-event-forecasting hits. ## [2026-06-29] research | Full LinkedIn + X people-sweep via live Chrome (Dannie-requested) — filed: 2 raw sources (raw/notes/). NEW in-window keepers: (1) raw/notes/dilger-harness-is-20-percent-requirements-are-80 (Martin Dilger, ~1h / 2026-06-29 — verbatim: building AI harnesses is "throwing technology at problems that can't be solved with technology alone"; the harness + code are only 20% of the solution, the other 80% is clarifying requirements / understanding business processes and getting them into a format that works; "nobody tells you how to write that damn markdown" — it's a human/communications problem with no technical solution; he focuses on the first 80% (Event Modeling), after which the rest "becomes pretty easy". On tight EM×agents focus — sharpens his recurring spec-clarity-over-harness thesis into an explicit 20/80 framing; ties [[harness-engineering]] ↔ [[event-modeled-agent-design]] ↔ [[spec-driven-development]]). (2) raw/notes/fritzsche-ccc-atomic-append-vs-serialized-write-order (Rico Fritzsche, ~6d / 2026-06-23 — verbatim: an atomic conditional append (PostgreSQL CTE combining Event Query check + insert) is NOT enough — under READ COMMITTED two concurrent commands can observe the same command context/version, reach identical decisions, and both append; Command Context Consistency needs a protected WRITE ORDER (serialization), e.g. locking a single metadata row per append txn — physical writes serialized globally, conflict decision stays local to the command context; "atomicity safeguards one append, serialization prevents two decisions from the same observed context being accepted"; the lock is an implementation detail, the contract is what matters. Concrete implementation layer under [[autonomous-domain-capabilities]] / [[dynamic-consistency-boundaries]] — fuller article linked in his first comment, not captured). FLAGGED not-filed (Fritzsche, in-window, on-thread but restatements of already-captured theses): "Clean Architecture is untouchable" critique (~1d / Jun-28 — Dependency Inversion doesn't remove functional deps; SoC should be by domain capability not horizontal layers; CRUD≠domain language; FC/IS as foundation; credits Ralf Westphal) + microservices-aren't-a-maturity-level critique (~5d / Jun-24, vs Anton Martyniuk's "modular monolith first") + "ES doesn't rely on DDD tactical patterns / aggregates were never required" (~1w / Jun-22 — same theme as the already-filed rico-fritzsche-es-does-not-require-aggregates-ccc-vs-dcb; his codewithrico X Jun-22 just links this blog article). ## [2026-07-02] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (lookback 14d, headless scheduled run) — filed: 1 raw source (raw/articles/enzler-event-sourcing-aggregates-dcb-or-what — Urs Enzler, planetgeek.ch, 2026-06-23, IN-WINDOW, verbatim via live Chrome after WebFetch returned an empty shell). NEW substrate keeper (Topic-2, anything-substantive) + a NEW practitioner voice not previously on the watch list: part 11 of Enzler's event-sourcing series — a pragmatic "third way" between DDD Aggregates and DCBs. Thesis: he watched conf talks making "a big fuss" about aggregates + discussed DCBs with Sara Pellegrini, but his team never needed either. Instead they DESIGN AWAY concurrency: (a) "small data" + task-based UI (a command per task, only the data that task needs, no unit-of-work) so collisions are near-impossible; (b) replace a concurrent design with a NON-concurrent one (student course-registration → draft-registration intent + a single-threaded batch algorithm that also enables prioritisation) — "solving concurrent consistency issues with non-concurrent designs"; (c) where serialisation IS needed, use Azure Service Bus SESSIONS (session-ID = tenant+employee+workday) to serialise validation within the consistency boundary, plus scheduled 5-min grace-period messages / dead-letter debugging / queue suspend under load. Framing: "solve problems when they arise, not earlier"; DCBs are "powerful but quite complicated", prefer simpler. Directly updates [[dynamic-consistency-boundaries]] (a skeptic/avoid-it-by-design counterweight to the Pellegrini/DCB + Fritzsche-CCC lines), [[event-sourcing]], [[cqrs]] (task-based UI / command-per-task); suggests entity urs-enzler. Ready to ingest. NOTE the wiki-relevant aside: he links consistency to LLMs ("wrong data is clearly bad. Why do I think of LLMs now?") but doesn't develop an agents angle — this is a substrate piece, not EM×agents. A reflection on the Event Modeling Conference 2026 (Munich, heatwave/Eisbach "is it safe to jump?" metaphor): the community does remarkable work but "struggles to tell a coherent story about itself" — fragmented across ES/CQRS/DDD/EM/Domain Storytelling; to grow, EM must "speak to leadership, not just engineers." States ChronosHub already has leadership buy-in for an EM+ES engineering strategy and a conviction its "architecture and strategy for the agentic future" are right; leaves an open question on how EM intersects with EXPERIENCE ENGINEERING — interaction experiences that work for both humans and AI agents. HIGH VALUE for Dannie (own org's public EM+ES+agentic strategy statement; the long-watched 2nd-EM-Conf angle, finally a practitioner write-up). Ready to ingest → suggest entities christian-johansen + chronoshub; touches [[event-modeled-agent-design]], [[event-modeling]], [[spec-driven-development]], and a new experience-engineering×agents seam. (2) raw/notes/dilger-local-llm-distributed-agent-setup-event-modeling (Martin Dilger, LinkedIn two-part series ~Jul 2-3; verbatim both parts). Concrete implementation report of his always-on distributed build factory: 3 on-prem Asus GX10 running local models (Gemma4 ~60 tok/s, Qwen3.6:27B ~30 tok/s) via Ollama + Claude Code/OpenCode (+ a Hermes agent); 6-10 agents in ralph-loops 24/7; board-driven — a slice moved to "Planned" is claimed+locked by exactly one agent → "In Progress" (the whole synchronization trick; no collisions, no merge conflicts, no coupling hell); comments/voice-to-text spawn Modeling Tasks; self-provisioning Build-Kits/Modeling-Kits with "batteries-included" per-stack skills; move a slice "Done"→"Planned" and an agent reconciles code to spec; focus shifts to the spec-side ("what" not "how"); "triplet of flexible architectures" = Event Modeling + Event Sourcing + Slice-Based. Enriches [[dilger-event-modeling-agent-harness]] + [[dilger-model-is-a-living-spec-always-on-agent]] with real hardware/models + the claim-lock mechanism. Ready to ingest. ## [2026-07-06] research | Live-Chrome LinkedIn + X people-sweep (Dannie-requested "full sweep", closing the headless run's flagged gap) — filed: 1 raw source. NEW in-window keeper: raw/articles/dilger-how-does-dcb-affect-event-modeling (Martin Dilger, LinkedIn Pulse / "Event Modeling applied" newsletter, 2026-07-06 — verbatim). On TIGHT EM×agents focus + DCB substrate. Thesis: after modeling several systems with DCB, "how does DCB affect Event Modeling? — it's not affected at all, quite the opposite, everything gets simpler." Key points: aggregates = "Static Consistency Boundaries" (set in stone by production, costly to change) vs DCB treats all events in a bounded context as one global stream, focus on "what actually happened"; with DCB you model ONE swimlane per system/bounded context and swimlanes revert to their true purpose — showing INTEGRATION between systems/teams (e.g. Payments = a different team's system), not stream-design; you do NOT separately model a "Decision Model" — the GIVEN part of the GWT scenarios supplies exactly the info a command handler needs; DCB makes decisions granular (command-by-command) — in Axon you define a per-handler Criteria (SQL-like query over event types for an id) and "we typically generate this code directly from the specifications — AI is really good at that — the Axon Build Kit for the Eventmodelers-Platform translates the Event Model to executable code" + generates the test cases; Tags are indices, NOT domain concepts — don't model them; use the publicly documented schema (reuse the id-attribute for now) so any code-generator/agent can plug in; two session types (Discovery = no tech details / Detailed-Modeling = add tags before handing the slice to an Agent). Directly updates [[dynamic-consistency-boundaries]], [[event-modeling]], [[given-when-then]], [[event-modeled-agent-design]], ties to [[dilger-dcb-is-what-event-sourcing-should-have-been]] (Static-vs-Dynamic CB), [[dilger-build-kits-model-to-generated-code]] (Axon Build Kit codegen), [[enzler-event-sourcing-aggregates-dcb-or-what]] + [[rico-fritzsche-es-does-not-require-aggregates-ccc-vs-dcb]] (the aggregate-vs-DCB debate), and the AxonIQ/[[allard-buijze]] model→code thread. Ready to ingest. RESOLVED the recurring flag: the "Agentic Event Modeling on Eventmodelers.ai | Spec-Driven" post (activity-7470769576843239424) that prior runs (07-04/07-05) flagged as the likely top in-window keeper turns out to be an EDITED RE-SHARE of the already-filed [[dilger-spec-driven-development-applied]] ("Prompt. ## [2026-07-12] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (lookback 14d, headless scheduled run) — filed: 1 raw source (raw/articles/addyosmani-own-the-outer-loop — Addy Osmani, "Own the Outer Loop", addyo.substack.com/Elevate, 2026-07-09, IN-WINDOW, verbatim via headless fetch; Osmani is watch-listed + lead loop-engineering voice). Config loaded fine (topics 1–4 + 15 people current). ONE KEEPER — Osmani's follow-on to his loop-engineering primary: as agents take the INNER execution loop, engineers must OWN THE OUTER LOOP (accountability). Three terms: Quality (back-pressure checks that produce evidence) → Verdict (the human ship/block/redirect production decision) → Answerability (guarantee you can explain WHY). Agent = model + harness (files/tools/memory/skills/sandboxes/permissions/observability/recovery) runs the loop (investigate→implement→verify→repeat), where an INDEPENDENT check (not the model's say-so) decides done; a factory = loops at scale. Humans belong in the constraints/sampling/audit/ownership loops, NOT the inner loop. Restates his three hidden costs (cognitive surrender / cognitive debt / orchestration tax) with fresh data: Sonar 2026 State of Code = 42% of committed code AI-generated/assisted; GitLab Jun-2026 = governance happens AFTER creation (bottleneck = review/validation); Anthropic RCT = AI-assisted engineers score 17pp lower on comprehension (50% vs 67%); Wharton = when AI was wrong ~75% accepted it anyway with MORE confidence. "Accountability contract" per codebase; "the agent can ship more than you can review"; alpha/decay/taste (Paul Graham + Hashimoto "taste = judgment where no metric exists yet"); "should this exist, can we answer for it?" is the new bottleneck. Directly extends [[loop-engineering]] (the inner/outer-loop framing + "stay the engineer"), ties to [[addyosmani-loop-engineering]] (same author, the Jun-7 primary this builds on), [[langchain-the-art-of-loop-engineering]] (maker≠checker/independent verification), [[willison-understand-to-participate]] + [[tornhill-ai-readable-code-series]] (comprehension/cognitive debt), [[bockeler-context-engineering-coding-agents]], [[agent-explainability]] (answerability↔why), and the harness/factory seam in [[fowler-agentic-programming]] + [[stripe-minions-one-shot-coding-agents]]. Ready to ingest → touches [[addy-osmani]], [[loop-engineering]], and the accountability/answerability seam (candidate new concept). -be-enough (Adam Tornhill, "Code for Humans and Machines" Substack, 2026-06-30 — search-INDEXING-LAG missed it this morning; surfaced via his LinkedIn share ~Jul-8, article read via live Chrome since Substack is client-rendered). Code-health × agentic-coding thread: even human-EXPERT-level coding agents won't be enough because AI itself RAISES the quality bar via (a) SCALE — bigger/more-complex systems (Lehman's laws) and (b) SPEED — orders-of-magnitude faster generation → higher churn/fault risk ("wrong at scale"); defect opportunities scale with code+change volume; LLMs' stochastic core means a one-in-a-million error recurs across millions of decisions. Rejects the "superhuman code quality" path (no training data/feedback signal; AGI perennially "a few years away"). Prescription: don't chase perfect code — "double down on creating environments where unreliable agents reliably produce acceptable outcomes" (accept imperfection, engineer the environment). Ties [[tornhill-ai-readable-code-series]], [[tornhill-codescene-unhealthy-code-agentic-token-cost]], [[harness-engineering]]/[[loop-engineering]] (environment-over-model), [[borg-tornhill-code-for-machines-not-just-humans]] (defect-risk empirics), and the verification-burden/maker≠checker seam. ## [2026-07-23] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (lookback 14d, headless scheduled run) — filed: 1 raw source (raw/articles/addyosmani-software-factories-light-and-dark — Addy Osmani, "Software Factories, Light and Dark", addyosmani.com, 2026-07-20, IN-WINDOW, verbatim via headless WebFetch; Osmani is watch-listed + lead loop-engineering voice). ONE KEEPER — the 07-22 run checked Osmani's blog and said "nothing newer than Jul-14", but he published a new post Jul-20 that GROWS the software-factory thread flagged since 07-12 (the Latent.Space AIEWF "software factories" dispatch marker). Content: the definitional loop→harness→factory stack ("the loop is the atom; the factory is the loop at scale"; a factory is "an org chart made of loops", not a bigger agent); LIGHT vs DARK factory — a dark factory ships code no human has read, verified only by machines (analogy: FANUC/Xiaomi lights-out manufacturing; "in software, the floor is the diff"), a lit factory keeps the lights on where judgment lives and moves human review UPSTREAM to product/design/architecture; "harness engineering is not enough" (leans on Dex Horthy/HumanLayer's AIEWF talk "Harness Engineering is not Enough: Why Software Factories Fail" + 12-factor-agents); comprehension debt as the debt a dark factory silently takes on "with the tests green the whole way"; BACK PRESSURE restated ("hand a loop as much autonomy as you can cheaply and reliably verify, and not one inch more"; verification, not generation, is the constraint — the wide-mouth/narrow-neck funnel); "what earns a loop the dark" = cheap + high-frequency + un-fakeable green/red oracle, 3–10 step short loops (Horthy's rule), keep lights on where a wrong answer is expensive; LOOPS vs GRAPHS vs STATE MACHINES — "owning your control flow is really just walking the graph back around the loop", cites LangGraph/LlamaIndex Workflows, Jerry Liu, David Khourshid — directly answers the "graph engineering" framing flagged in the 07-20 run and ties to [[nick-tune-graphs-memory-skills-agents]]; closes on the inner/outer-loop boundary (evidence = diffs/tests/logs). Directly extends [[loop-engineering]] (adds the factory rung + light/dark + graphs-vs-loops), builds on [[addyosmani-loop-engineering]], [[addyosmani-own-the-outer-loop]] (same author, inner/outer loop), [[addyosmani-earning-taste-and-judgment]]; ties to [[fowler-agentic-programming]] + [[stripe-minions-one-shot-coding-agents]] (factory/unattended agents), [[willison-understand-to-participate]] + [[tornhill-ai-readable-code-series]] (comprehension/cognitive debt), [[langchain-the-art-of-loop-engineering]] (maker≠checker/verification), [[bockeler-context-engineering-coding-agents]], and the "software-factory" marker first flagged 07-12. ## [2026-07-27] ingest   | AHE — Agentic Harness Engineering (observability-driven automatic harness evolution) — touched: 7 pages (source page ahe-agentic-harness-engineering NEW; concept loop-engineering +hill-climbing-loop-measured section + loop-4 primary ref + open-questions update + sources/updated; concept harness-engineering +self-evolving-surface practice bullet + open-questions update + sources/updated; concept agent-harness +NexAU seven-component enumeration + sources/updated; overview loop-engineering paragraph +AHE "light independent evidence now partly addressed" + frontmatter; index +1 source). No new entity created — authors are not recurring watch voices, referenced inline on the source page (per the Litt-inline precedent). Content: an evolution agent autonomously rewrites a coding agent's harness (base model FROZEN) via 3 observability pillars — component (decoupled 7-file substrate: system prompt / tool description / tool implementation / middleware / skill / sub-agent config / long-term memory, each failure→one file, git-commit rollback), experience (millions of trajectory tokens distilled to layered per-task root-cause + benchmark overview, progressive disclosure), decision (change manifest pairs each edit w/ predicted fixes+regressions, next-round attribution, per-edit rollback; verifier/model config read-only so the self-modifier can't cheat). Results: Terminal-Bench 2 pass@1 69.7→77.0% over 10 iters, beats hand-built Codex-CLI (71.9%) + self-evolve baselines ACE/TF-GRPO; gain STRUCTURAL not prose (memory-only +5.6 / tools +3.3 / middleware +2.2 pp, system-prompt-only −2.3 pp — so prompt-only self-evolution misses it); frozen harness transfers (SWE-bench-verified −12% tokens; +5.1..+10.1 pp across 3 model families, largest on weaker bases = environment substitutes for capability); honest "regression blindness" (predicts fixes ~5x random, regressions only ~2x). Ties [[loop-engineering]] (4th loop empirical primary), [[harness-engineering]]/[[agent-harness]] (harness as learnable surface + refined primitive list), [[agent-observability-and-evals]], [[context-engineering]], [[tornhill-why-human-level-ai-wont-be-enough]] (engineer-the-environment corroborated); sits with [[esaa-event-sourcing-for-autonomous-agents]] + [[borg-tornhill-code-for-machines-not-just-humans]] as the academic-corroboration strand. Caveat: single-benchmark controlled research prototype, high-variance, incomplete guardrail stack, real compute overhead. Clears the carried candidate-primary flag.

## [2026-07-27] research | Live-Chrome full sweep (Dannie-requested — STEP 1b social + login-walled people feeds, closing the gap pending since 07-18) — filed: 1 raw source (raw/articles/dilger-triplet-flexible-agent-enabled-architecture — Martin Dilger, LinkedIn newsletter "Event Modeling applied", 2026-07-26, verbatim full article via get_page_text). ONE KEEPER — Dilger's dedicated naming/write-up of "The Triplet": a flexible, agent-enabled architecture = Event Modeling (plan) + Vertical Slices (structure) + Event Sourcing (store), used in union; the same low coupling that makes it cheap for humans to change makes it cheap for AI agents (an agent works one slice, needs only that slice's context + event log + model, minimizing context/token/review cost; add agents → faster, not slower); coupling-as-the-disease / exponential cost curve; requirements are PART of the architecture (model-first, boundaries settled in the room not by whoever's loudest); "Slice-Cycle-Time" as an estimation alternative to story points; the eventmodelers.ai platform is built around the Triplet (Git-backed versioning, JSON import/export, Build-Kit codegen, MCP+skills). The definitive primary for the "triplet" only referenced in passing before ([[dilger-local-llm-distributed-agent-setup-event-modeling]], [[dilger-dcb-is-what-event-sourcing-should-have-been]]) and the full version of the previously-stubbed [[dilger-hold-my-beer-engineer]] "Event Modeling applied" teaser. ## [2026-07-29] research | Live-Chrome full sweep (Dannie-requested — STEP 1b social + login-walled people feeds, following the headless run earlier today) — filed: 1 raw source (raw/articles/adaptech-workflow-not-inside-giant-process-manager — Adaptech Group [Adam Dymitruk's consultancy], "Your Workflow Should Not Live Inside a Giant Process Manager", LinkedIn Pulse, 2026-07-28, verbatim full article via get_page_text; surfaced as a Dymitruk repost 9h ago). ONE KEEPER — a substantive EM/ES-substrate primary (Topic-2 method-wide + Topic-3 substrate): replace one large saga/process-manager with (a) a PROJECTED TO-DO LIST — a projection that records, per active process, what is known / done / unfinished / whether enough info exists to continue (stock-purchase worked example: requested stock+qty, price received?, price timestamp, order submitted?, submission result, retry allowed?) — the projection doesn't replace the event history, it turns it into a view people AND automated processors read; and (b) many FOCUSED single-responsibility processors observing that same to-do list (price-fetch / submit / retry-or-verify), each reacting only to its fields. Core claims: external calls are business STATES not procedure lines (not-sent / awaiting / arrived-too-late / done-but-unconfirmed → needs timestamps + idempotency keys); recovery should be visible BEFORE failure (event history + to-do list = durable evidence, verify-don't-recharge); business-language statuses (PaymentAcceptedAwaitingShipment > "instance at step 7") power dashboards/support/SLA; Event Modeling exposes this structure before implementation ("instead of one box labeled Saga", the timeline of commands/events/projections/automations shows it) — "at Adaptech Group, this is one reason Event Modeling is treated as a foundation for Event Sourcing work"; migration on-ramp = pick one confusing saga, list every datum it needs, ask which missing item blocks the next decision. Ties [[event-modeling]] (the projection/automation "TODO-list" pattern made explicit), [[event-sourcing]], [[cqrs]] (projection-as-read-model), [[vertical-slice-architecture]] (one processor = one responsibility/slice), [[adam-dymitruk]]/[[adaptech-group]]; a saga-vs-process-manager design primary the substrate lacked. Ready to ingest → touches [[event-modeling]], [[event-sourcing]], [[adaptech-group]], [[adam-dymitruk]], concept pages for projections/automations/process-managers. ## [2026-08-03] research | DORA ROI report capture (Dannie-directed do-next) — filed: 1 raw source (raw/articles/dora-roi-ai-assisted-software-development-2026 — InfoQ writeup of DORA "ROI of AI-Assisted Software Development" v2026.01, Google Cloud/DORA, retrieved verbatim). Closes the standing [[dora]] future-marker; grounds [[skelton-team-topologies-foundation-ai-roi]]. The PRIMARY is a gated Google Cloud lead-gen download (cloud.google.com/resources/content/dora-roi-of-ai-assisted-software-development) and dora.dev/ai/roi/report+calculator are content-free landing pages, so the reputable InfoQ secondary — which quotes the primary directly — is the most substantive fetchable record (supersede if the gated PDF is obtained). Key claims captured: AI-as-AMPLIFIER (returns come from the organizational system — internal-platform quality, workflow clarity, team alignment — not the tools, per Nathen Harvey); the J-CURVE of value realisation (a temporary productivity dip = learning curve + verification tax + downstream-process adaptation, "the tuition cost of transformation" — don't pull funding during the dip); ROI methodology = value flows through 7 capabilities → improved DORA delivery metrics → non-financial outcomes (DevEx/UX) → financial outcomes (cost savings/revenue), ROI=(value−investment)/investment; illustrative 500-eng org → ~$11.6M return vs $8.4M investment = 39% first-year ROI, ~8-month payback (flagged high-uncertainty "conversation starter"); INSTABILITY TAX (more code faster raises change-fail rate 5%→6%, sample −$344k downtime); Stanford finding 35–40% gain on greenfield vs ≤10% on complex legacy; agentic-era ROI reframed as "unlock latent human creativity by offloading systemic toil," discourages headcount reduction; Google Cloud 3-yr avg 727% ROI cited. Ready to ingest → would create the source page, promote the [[dora]] marker to a real entity, and touch [[business-capabilities]], [[skelton-team-topologies-foundation-ai-roi]], [[agentic-ai]]/[[agent-governance]], [[harness-engineering]] (verification tax). Caveat: vendor (Google Cloud) framing + captured via secondary.

## [2026-08-10] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (HEADLESS scheduled run, lookback 14d → since ~2026-07-27) — filed: 1 raw source. Ends a 10-run 0-filed streak (08-01→08-09). CAPTURED: Oskar Dudycz, "Fixing bugs in Event Sourcing is hard, for real?" (Architecture Weekly / event-driven.io, Jul-27-2026) → raw/articles/dudycz-fixing-bugs-in-event-sourcing.md — Topic-3 substrate keeper: fixing bad data in ES vs state-based. Core thesis: in a mutable/state model, a data-fix migration OVERWRITES the evidence you'd need to check whether the fix worked, so one migration ends up repairing the last one (attempt-3 computed from attempt-2's output); in ES the original events are immutable so every correction is computed from the booking, not from the prior fix. Practical primitives: (1) event `metadata.buildSha` = the commit that appended the event → query the exact rows a broken binary produced (900 real vs 340 merely-touched), plus correlationId/causationId to trace which request; (2) fix-forward via an appended corrective event (`ReservationPriceCorrected`, the accountant's correcting-entry move) — never edit events in place ("you should not change the past... even including bugs is a valid scenario"); (3) make correction a first-class REQUIREMENT/command (`CorrectReservationPrice` w/ permissions + mandatory reason) so the bulk fix runs through the same command+events as a front-desk operator (nobody `UPDATE`s prod at 23:00); (4) "the customer already fixed it" — a blind bulk recalc silently UNDOES a human's deliberate goodwill correction, so skip streams where a price-affecting event follows the bad one. Links out to his own [[event-versioning-and-upcasting]]-adjacent "Simple patterns for events schema versioning" + "No, it can never happen!". Reached via the Architecture Weekly Substack mirror (fetchable) — event-driven.io itself is client-rendered; this is why the 08-05→08-09 headless runs missed it. Ready to ingest (touches [[event-sourcing]], [[oskar-dudycz]], [[event-driven-architecture]], new concept candidate: corrective/compensating events + build-SHA event metadata). (martinfowler.com, "Exploring Gen AI" series, published/modified Aug-10-2026, announced Aug-11) → raw/articles/bockeler-tdd-inside-the-agent-loop.md — a tracked-person primary squarely on the loop-engineering / verification thread and directly adjacent to the KB's [[given-when-then]]-as-acceptance-gate line. Core: an exploratory eval (5 batches; small/medium/large greenfield business-logic tasks; Sonnet 4.6 generating, Sonnet 4.6 judging TDD adherence, Opus 4.8 blind-ranking solutions + mutation scores) of whether instructing an agent to do TDD *fully inside its own loop* helps. TLDR: no discernable quality difference, and non-TDD runs were ranked slightly HIGHER on design/test quality more often than not; no meaningful mutation-score difference; TDD cost ~3–8.5x the tokens (small 8.50x, medium 2.96x, large 4.89x — caveated as directional, cache-read-inflated). Mechanism hypothesis (from Opus reading the traces): non-TDD and test-first runs did FULL UP-FRONT DESIGN (architecture, data types, edge cases, contracts) before any code; TDD instructions actively work against that, so design emerged from locally-minimal decisions, was rarely revisited, and locked into whatever shape the first test took — "behaviour the agent didn't think to write a test for didn't get implemented at all". Ivett Ördög's training-data theory quoted: models have seen finished functions + descriptions, almost no step-by-step TDD, so their internal representation is requirements→code, not the process of getting there. Goal-by-goal audit of what TDD stops buying you once the human leaves the loop: red-green proves nothing when the same agent writes the test AND confirms the failure ("a red test tells you the agent ran it and saw failure, not that the failure was for the right reason"); tautological self-checking tests still appeared despite test-first; YAGNI/small-steps and Beck's "managing fear" are human-centred benefits that don't transfer; agents overshoot minimal implementations because they already hold the whole spec. Her conclusions — being overly specific about HOW a model should work is not sustainable; instead monitor OUTCOMES and automate feedback, and think carefully about where the human inserts themselves as arbiter; she has stopped telling coding agents to do TDD. Proposed substitutes: mutation testing as the regression sensor, static analysis + periodic AI modularity review + files-touched/tokens-per-change trends as refactoring triggers (all cross-linking her captured [[fowler-bockeler-maintainability-sensors]]), and Ivett Ördög's "Approved Scenarios" (frozen, human-approved functional scenarios in a bespoke runner) as a confidence mechanism. Ready to ingest — touches [[birgitta-bockeler]], [[harness-engineering]], [[loop-engineering]], [[maintainability-sensors]], [[given-when-then]], [[agentic-coding]], [[vibe-modeling]]; note the TENSION it creates with the KB's GWT/spec-first through-line (Dilger/Dymitruk hold that the *model's* GWT specs govern the agent — Böckeler's finding is about the agent inventing its own tests step-by-step inside the loop, i.e. it argues against agent-authored micro-TDD, NOT against human/model-authored acceptance specs; worth stating explicitly on the concept page). ## [2026-08-15] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (HEADLESS scheduled run, lookback 14d → since ~2026-08-01) — filed: 2 raw sources (1 in-window keeper + 1 deliberate out-of-window BACKFILL). Effective new span ~1 day since the 08-14 live-Chrome sweep + full ingest. Config loaded clean (topics 1–4 + 15 people current). CAPTURED (1) **in-window keeper**: Oskar Dudycz, "Vertical slices, their ownership and external dependencies" (event-driven.io, **2026-08-10**) → raw/articles/dudycz-vertical-slices-ownership-and-external-dependencies.md. Topic-3 substrate, and the single best answer the KB now holds to the question the [[vertical-slice-architecture]] page leaves open — *what does a slice do when it needs something it doesn't own?* Core: **everything outside the slice is external** (the next folder, the parent module, another module, a third-party API, the database — one category); the slice **declares a narrow function type in its own vocabulary** (`CheckContractor = (id) => Promise<'Active'|'Unknown'|'Suspended'>`) rather than importing a wide provider interface, and composition happens by **hand-rolled partial application in one file near the entry point** (no container, no registration, no lifetime scopes). Vocabulary correction the KB has needed: **slice = one piece of functionality cut through the whole app, "more a function than an entity"; module = a logical grouping of slices (criterion: what changes together); bounded context = a LINGUISTIC barrier, not a folder** — "seven features of one application are almost always slices, or at most modules, sitting inside a single context", and frontend/backend are two deployment targets, not two contexts; he says outright he doesn't love the term "bounded context" and would rather teams "talk about which functionalities share a vocabulary". Also: **module `api.ts` (what a module OFFERS) and the consumer-declared need (what a slice ASKS FOR) are opposite directions across the same boundary** and you want both, with the composition root as the only code that knows both vocabularies; **module-level cycles dissolve** because neither module imports the other (each declares its own narrow need) — "most module-level cycles I've run into were a shared concept whose owner hadn't been decided yet", and declaring narrow needs lets you defer that ownership decision instead of resolving it early and wrong; **structural/duck typing** (Go's consumer-declared interfaces, TS structural typing) is the enabling mechanism; **persistence split three ways — business logic per entity/aggregate, read model per query, database schema per MODULE** (a slice is a feature, not a persistence boundary; "a schema per slice gives you migrations that correspond to nothing"); two entry points (dispatcher UI + carrier event) live side by side in `confirming-order/` sharing the pure business logic but NOT the application logic, because each declares only what it loads; reuse policies/calculators, let handlers duplicate; don't force a 1:1 UI↔backend mapping (compose at the page, or write an honestly-named BFF slice); Greg Young's Task-Based UI cited for why a generic `status` endpoint loses intent ("the system records that the order is verified. It doesn't record that anybody verified it"). **Agents angle (his closing section, "the cherry on top"):** the same locality argument now doubles as a context-window argument — "the same property determines how much can fit in a context window and how much of a codebase an agent has to read before it can safely change one behaviour. A layered structure that needs five folders touched for one feature costs an agent the same way it costs us, faster… it's an old argument that happens to have got more valuable." That is a direct independent corroboration of [[miller-codebase-is-the-prompt-vertical-slices-ai]] and [[locality-of-reference]] from a non-.NET, non-vendor voice. Ready to ingest — touches [[vertical-slice-architecture]], [[oskar-dudycz]], [[locality-of-reference]], [[cqrs]], [[domain-driven-design]], [[business-capabilities]], [[balanced-coupling]], [[coupling-taxonomy]], [[ai-readable-code]], [[event-sourcing]] (offered as optional, "nothing above depends on it"). Note it was missed by the 08-13/08-14 runs because event-driven.io is Gatsby/client-rendered on the index — the *article* URL fetches fine headless once a search surfaces it. CAPTURED (2) **out-of-window backfill, flagged for Dannie's accept/reject**: Kief Morris (Thoughtworks), "Humans and Agents in Software Engineering Loops" (martinfowler.com, "Exploring Gen AI" series, **2026-03-04**) → raw/articles/morris-humans-and-agents-in-software-engineering-loops.md. Surfaced while checking Böckeler's series index and found to be a **missing antecedent primary for the KB's whole Thread-4**: it splits the **"why loop" (idea → working software, humans run it) from the "how loop" (intermediate artefacts: code, tests, tooling, ADRs)**, notes the how loop is really nested loops (feature → story → code), and then names the three human positions — **outside the loop** (= vibe coding, and "some interpretations of Spec Driven Development"), **in the loop** (human as per-line gatekeeper in the innermost loop → "we become a bottleneck… humans spending more time specifying and reviewing code than they save"), and **ON the loop** (build and manage the harness instead) — with the sharpest one-line test in the literature: *"The 'in the loop' way is to fix the artefact… The 'on the loop' way is to change the harness that produced the artefact so it produces the results we want."* It also names **"the agentic flywheel"** — an agent harness that generates recommendations for improving itself, fed by tests/evals then pipeline stages, production operational data, user-journey logs and commercial results, with a maturity path from interactive review → backlog items → auto-approval above a risk/cost/benefit score — and closes on the honest observation that at the limit this "might look a lot like humans out of the loop, old-school vibe coding… for standard types of work". WHY IT MATTERS FOR THE WIKI: it predates by four months and independently anticipates [[addyosmani-own-the-outer-loop]] (Jul-15, "agents take the inner loop, humans own the outer loop") and LangChain's fourth stacked "hill-climbing" loop in [[langchain-the-art-of-loop-engineering]], and it links "on the loop" to the "middle loop" coinage from Fowler's Future of Software Development Retreat — so [[loop-engineering]] and [[harness-engineering]] currently under-credit their own venue. It also supplies the missing *internal*-quality argument for [[ai-readable-code]] ("we care about internal quality when it affects external outcomes… when LLMs can more quickly understand and modify the code they work faster and spiral less") and a careful footnote that colloquial "ralph loop" usage has drifted from Ghuntley's original, where **the operator plays an important role in steering** — relevant to [[ralph-loop]] and to Dilger's 24/7 ralph-loop harness. Ingest only if Dannie accepts an out-of-window backfill; if not, delete the raw file. ## [2026-08-16] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (HEADLESS scheduled run, lookback 14d → since ~2026-08-02) — filed: 1 raw source (0 in-window; 1 deliberate out-of-window BACKFILL, 4 days outside the window). Effective new span ~1 day since the 08-15 run. Config loaded clean (topics 1–4 + 15 people current). CAPTURED (1) **out-of-window backfill, flagged for Dannie's accept/reject**: Laurie Voss, "What the Hell Is a Loop, Anyway?" (O'Reilly Radar, **2026-07-29**, originally published on LinkedIn) → raw/articles/voss-what-the-hell-is-a-loop-anyway.md. This is the **disambiguation primary the [[loop-engineering]] page has been missing**, and it directly answers the standing flag raised on 08-13/08-14/08-15 (*"the loops-vs-graphs vocabulary is consolidating without us and we have no tracked primary voice"*) — not by covering graph engineering, but by doing the more useful thing: showing that "loop" itself already means **at least four different architectures**, and naming each with its own iteration unit, exit condition and human role. The four: (1) **execution loop** — the agent's own act→observe→decide cycle; iterates on steps within one task; ends on environment feedback (test output, API response, file contents); "also ends whenever the agent decides it's done, whether or not it actually is" — the failure that motivated wrapping it. (2) **task loop** — Huntley's **Ralph loop**; restart the agent against the same spec with a *completely fresh context window every iteration*, exactly one task per loop; "the apparent waste is the point: refeeding the full spec each time prevents the context rot and compaction events that quietly degrade long-running sessions"; iterates on a single artifact, ends on spec compliance + passing tests; Huntley's human is a **"locomotive engineer"** whose job is watching the loop, spotting failure patterns and fixing them so they never recur. (3) **product loop** — the **software factory**; Zach Lloyd (Warp) enumerates the lifecycle as triage → specification → implementation → review → verification → shipping → monitoring, "software engineering becomes factory engineering… you'll be building the thing that builds the product", with a concrete adoption path (start on low-risk repos, ratchet the automatic PR-merge rate from ~20% toward 60%); iterates on a codebase + backlog *continuously*, closing signals come from **outside** the codebase (new issues, production logs, user feedback, review outcomes); Anthropic's Claude Tag at 65% of product-team code cited as the same experiment. (4) **system loop / "autoresearch"** (Roland Gavrilescu, Introspection) — the inner loop does user-facing work, the outer loop *studies and maintains the primary system*, iterating on prompts, harnesses, model choices **and the evals themselves**; "the loop is the product"; two existence proofs — **Karpathy's ~630-line autoresearch (Mar 2026, 50 hypothesis→edit→evaluate experiments overnight on one GPU)** and Meta's Brain2Qwerty v2 (agents iteratively rewrote the codebase to invent better decoding architectures) — with the honest caveat that "final training configurations were still selected by hand. Even the flagship system loop keeps a human at the last checkpoint." TWO FURTHER CONTRIBUTIONS: (a) Voss **names the "???? loop"** left unlabelled at the top of swyx's own Loopcraft diagram — the **oversight loop** ("set goals, allocate, cull"; exit condition: none), "the one ring where a human should live", quoting Osmani from the AIEWF stage: *"That inner loop is capability. The outer loop is agency."*; (b) a **negative definition** that tightens the vocabulary — Cognition's Devin Security Swarm "Agentic MapReduce" is **not** a loop: "dispatch, gather, validate is a pipeline: nothing feeds back into a next cycle, and **a loop without feedback is just a for statement**"; fan-out is a *topology* deployable inside any of the four loops. Also the cleanest single account the KB would hold of the **AIEWF closing debate** (Jul 2): Dex Horthy ("not anti-loop — Kubernetes is built on control loops, but **deterministic** ones"; enthusiasm has outrun the engineering; step *down* an abstraction level, not up) vs Geoffrey Litt (loops are inevitable; "those who delegate understanding get replaced by the agent"), with Mike Krieger's data point that even inside Anthropic the Tag team is "bottlenecked on reviews and on the human ability to conceptualize what the system is doing" — i.e. **the checkpoint humans kept for themselves is now the constraint**. WHY IT MATTERS: the KB currently collapses all four of these into one [[loop-engineering]] page plus [[software-factory]]; Voss's map lets those pages separate *which* loop each captured primary is actually about ([[willison-designing-agentic-loops]] and [[anthropic-getting-started-with-loops]] = execution/task; [[dilger-local-llm-distributed-agent-setup-event-modeling]] and [[ralph-loop]] = task; [[addyosmani-software-factories-light-and-dark]] and [[stripe-minions-one-shot-coding-agents]] = product; [[ahe-agentic-harness-engineering]] and LangChain's hill-climbing loop = system), and it supplies a *name* for the human ring that [[addyosmani-own-the-outer-loop]] and [[willison-directly-responsible-individuals]] both circle without naming. It is a secondary/synthesis piece by a non-tracked author (Voss is Arize DevRel and the closing paragraph carries a vendor plug — noted in the capture), but it is the only item found in three sweeps that organizes the vocabulary rather than re-circulating it. **Ingest only if Dannie accepts an out-of-window backfill; if not, delete the raw file.** STEP 1b (X + LinkedIn social-keyword sweep) SKIPPED (headless; login-walled + client-rendered per watch-config `_social_topic_search`/`_x_coverage`) — last full live-Chrome sweep 08-14 (2 days ago), so **not yet stale, but this is the second consecutive headless run** and a live sweep is the only channel for Dilger/Dymitruk/Tornhill/Khononov/Nick Tune/Goeleven/Fraktalio. ## [2026-08-16] ingest   | Ng — "Spec-Driven Development Is Waterfall in Markdown" (Dannie-directed) — touched: 12 pages. NEW source page (1): ng-spec-driven-development-is-waterfall-in-markdown. NEW entities (2): **alvis-ng** (technical lead / Medium essayist; not a tracked voice — entered via this one ingest) and **gojko-adzic** (stub; BDD pioneer per the source's own description, present only as a secondhand quote, flagged as a real gap given how much [[given-when-then]] leans on that tradition). NEW concept (1): **decision-trace** — Ng's counter-proposal to the up-front spec (recorded cross-functional sync → LLM-structured notes → ticket → human-written prompt → agent-maintained decision log), distinguished from an ADR by *direction* (accumulates during the work rather than describing it beforehand) and framed as a debugging instrument: a stale spec fails silently, a trace fails legibly. UPDATED concepts (5): spec-driven-development (major — new section carrying the external evidence + the interface-not-quality mechanism, and its standing "production case study" open question now **partially answered against the thesis**), event-modeled-agent-design (new section "whose model is it?" — the flattening objection is the sharpest external challenge to the page, and the KB has no evidence modelling sessions resist it), given-when-then (new "provenance condition" — Böckeler asks who *authored* the test, Ng asks who *agreed* to it), comprehension-debt ("reviewer, not builder" as the debt at the moment it's taken on), context-engineering (a third failure mode beside rot and pollution: **context that was never captured** — social, not textual). UPDATED entity (1): birgitta-bockeler (the Fowler/Böckeler **Kiro** analysis Ng cites is NOT in the KB — flagged as an uncaptured primary, likely in the Exploring Gen AI series). index.md +1 source +2 entities +1 concept; overview.md +1 frontmatter source, a new passage in the focus-area thread extending the mid-August counter-weight cluster, and a new "Open questions" bullet listing the four uncaptured primaries. KEY CONTENT: (a) the KB's **first sustained outside-in critique of SDD** and the first source assembling its external empirical record — Scott Logic's SpecKit trial on a real production codebase (2,500 lines of Markdown in the spec phase incl. a 406-line research doc restating their own stack, bugs surviving, **~10x slower** than iterative prompting, "not a viable process… in its purest form"), the Fowler/Böckeler Kiro analysis (4 user stories / 16 acceptance criteria for a minor bug fix, "a sledgehammer to crack a nut", agents ignoring the spec anyway), Augment Engineer (1,300 lines of Markdown to display a date — "we've reinvented Big Design Up Front… replaced Word documents with Markdown and project managers with LLMs"), Marmelab ("The Waterfall Strikes Back"), and Gojko Adzic ("the revenge of waterfall or BDD taken to a new level"); market context = SpecKit 77K stars, Kiro >250K preview devs, Tessl $125M at a $750M valuation still in beta, 30+ frameworks by Mar-2026. (b) The mechanism is an **interface** argument, not a quality one — "write better specs" misses it: an agent "produces code that matches the words, not the intent", cannot ask whether the designer agreed to the flow, and a spec **flattens designers/DevOps/product into one voice, the author's** → *"the spec is a contract between you and the LLM that nobody else signed"*; plus spec rot changing character under agents (a stale spec used to be annoying, now it is executed confidently and fast, with no drift flag). (c) Filed explicitly as a **critique of document-first SDD toolkits, not of the KB's model-first thesis** — Ng's critique of those toolkits lands close to [[dilger-spec-driven-tools-need-event-modeling-front-half|Dilger's own complaint]] that they "skip the digging" (Dilger's phrase, not Ng's), and Ng and Dilger's "describing ≠ designing" converge on the same worry about the human checking out. The genuine collision is over **authorship**, which is now a named gap on event-modeled-agent-design. Caveats carried onto every page: all figures secondhand (no primary in raw/), his own SDD trial was a solo personal project and the team failure is reasoned not observed, the replacement workflow is unevaluated, and step 5 (agent logs its own decisions) reintroduces the self-reporting problem Böckeler raises. NEXT: capture the four underlying primaries (Scott Logic post is the one Ng links; the Fowler/Böckeler Kiro piece; Marmelab; anything by Adzic).

Found by fetching the **eventmodelers.ai `/docs/blog` index**, which — checking back through the log — the recent headless sweeps had NOT been hitting (they checked eventmodeling.org, the podcast index, and Dilger's LinkedIn via live Chrome, but not his own blog index). The index is otherwise current against the KB (Jul-26 Triplet = captured [[dilger-triplet-flexible-agent-enabled-architecture]]; Jul-6 DCB = captured [[dilger-how-does-dcb-affect-event-modeling]]; May-18 Hold My Beer = captured), so this is the **single missing article**, not a backlog. **RECOMMEND adding `https://www.eventmodelers.ai/docs/blog` to Dilger's `blog` list in watch-config.json** — it is headless-fetchable, weekly, and authored (unlike his LinkedIn, which needs live Chrome). CONTENT: the method-wide answer to a recurring practitioner question ("how do you model filtering?") — **UI-only interactions typically involve NO Command and NO Event**: "Filtering, sorting, expanding a row, switching a tab - these are all views on data you already have. Model them as Views, not as Commands looking for an Event to justify them." The worked example uses **Multi-Screen Views** (Page 1 = unfiltered book list, Page 2 = same `Books[]` Read Model with a filter field, both backed by the *exact same* read model — "no round trip to a Command, no new Event") plus the platform's **HTML View** editor, and states the behaviour as a GWT Scenario using the **built-in Query support** (Given two `Book registered` events, When query by title "Harry Potter", Then the `Books` read model returns the one match) — "Nothing about this Scenario depends on the UI plumbing. It's stated purely in terms of the data." WHY IT MATTERS FOR THE WIKI: (a) it is the **first worked instance of the read-side WHEN** the KB captured as a proposal in [[dilger-extending-event-modeling-query-when]] (Munich-conf feedback, shipped optional) — that page currently holds the *announcement*, this holds the *usage*; (b) it is a concrete **anti-pattern boundary** for [[event-modeling-anti-patterns]] / "The Shapes" — inventing "state changes that don't exist in the domain" to justify a screen interaction, i.e. the opposite failure to the ones read off a board's silhouette; (c) it strengthens [[agent-readable-model-artifacts]] with two new rungs: the **HTML mockup is "also accessible for a connected agent building from the model"** (the render-side complement to [[event-modeling:screen-mockups]]-style attachment and to [[dilger-highlighting-markers-give-context-to-agents]]), and Dilger names a **new npm CLI — `npx @eventmodelers/cli init-modeling`, "connecting your agent with the new CLI to the platform… only 15 seconds"** — a *third* agent-attachment channel alongside the MCP server and the ESDM/EmLang exports already captured in [[dilger-event-modeling-knowledge-hub-emlang]] / [[dilger-eventmodelers-supports-esdm-export]]. ## [2026-08-17] research | LIVE-CHROME sweep (Dannie-requested "do a full sweep"; covers STEP 1b social + the login-walled/client-rendered feeds the 08-15/08-16 headless runs flagged pending) — filed: 9 raw sources, ALL IN-WINDOW. Browser confirmed logged in (Windows, 1 local instance). The headless runs called it "a quiet week"; the live sweep shows that was a **channel artefact, not a real lull** — 5 of the 9 keepers live only on LinkedIn, and 2 more (arXiv) only surfaced via an X keyword search. FILED — Dilger ×4 (raw/notes/): (1) **"The lights-off software factory is a dead end, if you ask me"** (2026-08-16) → dilger-lights-off-software-factory-dead-end.md. The Event-Modeling camp's direct answer to [[addyosmani-software-factories-light-and-dark]], and the KB's first *practitioner* account of how to escape the review bottleneck without going dark: "you can´t do full code reviews - it´ll not be sustainable with the increased output - you just moved the bottleneck. But you can´t do no code-reviews either." His answer is **layers of trust**: Event Model as the spec → GWT scenarios → executable specifications ("call it Test-First Development if you will, as we define the tests long before any code") → then a **static-check gate whose four rules are the interesting part** — *were any tests adjusted* (review), *are there tests outside the specification* (review), ***were files changed outside the SUD (Slice Under Development) — immediate fail***, *were dependencies changed* (review) → then a **2–3 minute structural** manual review that is explicitly *not* functional ("at this point we already know it works. I look at the structure, does it match my mental map?"). Failure discards everything, **records a learning "why"**, and moves the slice back to "planned". Two reasons he keeps the short human review: staying connected to the codebase ("it allows me to keep a map of it in my head. If something goes wrong, I roughly know where to look") and catching structural problems cheaply. The load-bearing asymmetry: "I don´t care too much *how* a slice is implemented internally, as any bad implementation can be easily replaced. I do care a lot about the overall structure of the system and **the shape of the persisted data ( our Events )**. If that is broken, we got a serious issue - anything else can be easily fixed." Closes by naming the whole thing the **"Triple-Architecture"** (Spec-Driven Development via Event Modeling + Structure via Slices + Implementation via Event Sourcing) — note this is the same object as "the Triplet" in [[dilger-triplet-flexible-agent-enabled-architecture]] under a *second name*, worth reconciling on the concept page. (2) **"Trust needs to be engineered ( more than ever with AI )"** (2026-08-15) → dilger-trust-needs-to-be-engineered.md. The strongest [[vibe-modeling]] data point since the flea-market post: a **non-engineer** ("probably never wrote a line of code before") driving the boards entirely via AI + MCP, modelling complete requirements and generating working code — "I'm already building the slices." Anticipates the objection ("that´s just vibe coding, good luck in production") and concedes the premise — "Code generated by an agent left to its own devices is often crap… **it doesn't need to be wrong often to be desastrous.** One tiny dependency in the wrong place, a little too much coupling in another, and that's the start of a downward spiral in quality that is hard to stop." Then the thesis: "**AI can write good code. The thing is, you just can´t trust it to do so reliably. The real issue is trust.**" And the conclusion that pairs with (1): "Modeling upfront… that's not bureaucracy. It's engineering trust… That's how you work around the review bottleneck. **Not by doing more reviews, but by making most of them obsolete.**" Ends with the evidential claim — "Not a benchmark, not a demo. A non-developer shipping something real." (3) **"Mastering TODO Lists in Event Modeling was hard"** (2026-08-15) → dilger-todo-lists-storylines-one-scenario.md. A **new named EM artefact shipped the same day**: "**Storylines**" (formerly "Vertical Specs" — renamed because the old name confused people) — instead of several GWT scenarios per slice, *one* scenario describing the whole thing, "much more compact", chosen specifically because of the constraint "how to describe the behavior in a way it´s easy to understand by **human and AI alike**". Touches [[given-when-then]], [[process-managers-and-todo-lists]] (his TODO-list framing is the same object as Dymitruk's projected to-do list in [[adaptech-workflow-not-inside-giant-process-manager]]) and [[eventmodelers-ai]]. (4) **voice-to-sketch API** (2026-08-14) → dilger-voice-to-sketch-api-event-sourced-board.md. A new EM×agents *input* modality (spoken/dictated board construction, run on Qwen3.6:27B + Claude Code behind vLLM — note the local-model stack from [[dilger-local-llm-distributed-agent-setup-event-modeling]]), plus a genuinely novel wiki-relevant incident: **the agent consulted the board's own event history and pre-emptively disclaimed a deletion it hadn't made** — "the board's event history shows that right after our last round of edits, everything got deleted. I didn't issue those deletes myself." — "good we are event sourced". That is [[agent-explainability]] arriving from the *model board* side rather than the event store side, and the first instance in the KB of an agent using an event log to defend its own record. Dymitruk ×1 (raw/notes/): (5) **"Move as much from your prompts and agent md files into scripts"** (2026-08-15) → dymitruk-move-prompts-into-scripts-deterministic.md. Four sentences, but the claim is the convergence thesis stated at its most compact: "**Deterministic behaviour is your goal.** Evidence of how things work should be intermediate text files in directories that correspond to steps in your processes - even inboxes and outboxes. **You'll naturally arrive at #EventModeling and #EventSourcing.**" Pairs with (1)/(2) and with [[dymitruk-ai-melts-barrier-event-modeling-is-the-map]]; also a direct counter-current to the prompt/markdown-centric harness literature ([[bockeler-context-engineering-coding-agents]]) — move it *out* of prose and into scripts. NEW VOICE — Nick Tune ×1 (raw/articles/): (6) **"Enforced application architecture for agents and humans"** (nick-tune.me, **2026-08-13**) → nick-tune-enforced-application-architecture-agents-humans.md. **The most substantial single capture of this sweep**, and the [[fitness-functions]] page's missing worked primary. Thesis: "One of the most frustrating parts of AI-generated code is that it does not follow architectural guidelines that are written in skill files, ADRs, and various other places in the repo" — so **stop writing guidance and fail the build instead**. His line is the sharpest statement of the enforce-don't-document position the KB holds: "**AI does dumb stuff like that all the time even with a million lines of markdown screaming at it not to do that.**" The mechanism is a three-tier DSL (his tool, **Rivière**) over an NX/TypeScript monorepo: **package classification** (`packages/{subdomain}/domain-model` may contain only `/domain`, may only import `published-language`; a domain-model outside a subdomain folder = build failure), **layer rules** (folder→folder import permissions), and **role rules as a last resort** — code is annotated `/** @riviere-role … */` with roles (aggregate, value-object, domain-event, domain-port, domain-service, domain-error) so that e.g. `/adapters` may import *only* `domain-port` from `/domain`, and `/data-access` only `aggregate` and `value-object` but never `domain-service`, "because that type of coupling should never happen". Plus **role shape constraints** (value-object must be a class, private constructor, `parse`-prefixed static, branded private member, forbidden deps on aggregate/domain-service) whose purpose is explicitly anti-agent-drift: "**AI cannot implement an aggregate inside value-object because an aggregate-repository must return an aggregate. So it wouldn't be able to load it.**" Three further points that matter for the wiki: his use-cases package is **vertical slices by construction** (`/features/{feature}` with `/commands`, `/queries`, `/data-access`, `/adapters`, and `importRules: { allow: {} }` — no sharing between feature folders), i.e. an *enforced* version of [[vertical-slice-architecture]] and independent corroboration of [[dudycz-vertical-slices-ownership-and-external-dependencies]]'s "everything outside the slice is external"; he keeps **ADR-002 and the executable Rivière config deliberately aligned** ("Human-readable version: ADR-002 describes the same architecture for humans"), which is the two-representations answer to [[dilger-is-code-still-the-source-of-truth]]; and his honest cost accounting — package/domain/layer rules are cheap and "I find it hard to justify not adding those… If you can't set codebase standards at that level, I don't think you'll get far with agentic AI", whereas full role annotation is "still playing around with that. Come back in 6 months." Also names the evolutionary benefit: "Every time AI finds a way to write dumb code, I have the ability to add small constraints and prevent it" — the hill-climbing/harness-rewriting loop applied to *architecture rules* rather than prompts. Touches [[fitness-functions]], [[agent-legibility]], [[ai-readable-code]], [[vertical-slice-architecture]], [[domain-driven-design]], [[balanced-coupling]], [[nick-tune]], [[loop-engineering]], and is the natural companion to [[dilger-keep-command-handlers-pure]]. **CONFIG FIX NEEDED: watch-config lists Nick Tune with `blog: []` — he has an active blog at nick-tune.me/blog/ which is plain-HTML and headless-fetchable.** Adding it would have caught this in the 08-16 headless run. Latent.Space ×1 (raw/articles/): (7) **MacManus interviewing Fred Schott, "React for Agents: Astro Creator Brings Hooks to his Meta-Harness, Flue"** (2026-08-15) → macmanus-schott-react-for-agents-flue-meta-harness.md. **Resolves the standing "Omnigent / meta-harness" flag** and supplies the strongest vendor-neutral statement of the harness-as-constitutive position: "**Our early bet was that the harness is actually not a feature, but it's fundamental to what you think an agent is. There is no agent without a harness.**" Also maps the current framework layer for the KB: Flue 2 (Cloudflare/Schott, built on **Pi**, "an open source minimal harness" — Pi:Flue :: Vite:Astro) and Vercel's **eve** both treat the harness as built-in, versus the "OG agent frameworks" (Vercel AI SDK, Cloudflare Agents SDK, Mastra) now bolting harnesses on as a feature. The React analogy is load-bearing: an agent is a JS function that "**re-renders on every turn**", with 16 built-in hooks (`useSkill()`, `useTool()`, `useSubagent()`) letting agents "attach different resources and capabilities dynamically to enhance themselves at runtime" — i.e. the five primitives of [[addyosmani-loop-engineering]] reappearing as a *composition API*, and Bret Taylor's "we're sort of in the jQuery era of agents, not the react era" quoted as the framing. Confirms **meta-harness is still an unsettled term** ("there's confusion about what the term meta-harness even means at this early stage") and names two instances to watch — **Databricks' Omnigent** and **Exo, a self-improving harness** (Latent.Space interview with Exo coauthor Alex Krentsel forthcoming). Also a nice harness-eats-itself detail: Flue began as an issue-triage automation inside the Astro repo and became "like Claude Code, but 100% headless and programmable". arXiv ×2 (raw/papers/) — both surfaced *only* by the X keyword sweep on "agent harness", then confirmed against the arXiv API (the API listing shows **~25 "agent harness" papers in the last two weeks alone**, a signal the academic tier of Thread-4 is now moving faster than the blog tier): (8) **"Evo-Bench: Can Language Models Improve Agent Harness?"** (Huang et al., arXiv:2608.09096, 2026-08-10/11) → evo-bench-can-language-models-improve-agent-harness.md. The **benchmark counterpart to [[ahe-agentic-harness-engineering]]**: "the first benchmark designed to evaluate models' intrinsic harness-evolving capabilities", explicitly built to *isolate harness improvement from base-model strength* (harness-guided construction: auxiliary-task evolution picks tasks genuinely sensitive to framework changes, then sensitivity-aware stratified splitting for cross-suite generalisation). Findings that bear directly on [[loop-engineering]]'s hill-climbing loop: top models gain up to **16.6 absolute points, "closely approaching state-of-the-art human-engineered baselines"**; autonomous evolution **beats** hand-built harnesses on General and excels on Search but **struggles on Office tasks "that demand highly specific processing workflows"** (i.e. the loop fails exactly where the domain has idiosyncratic process — which is the Event Modeling wheelhouse); it exposes "critical temporal anomalies like **early saturation**"; and the synthesised harnesses "act as **highly transferable reasoning structures**", boosting other policy models — corroborating AHE's cross-model transfer claim from an independent group. (9) **"SBCO: Self-Supervised, Verifier-Grounded Harness Optimization For Planning Agents"** (Kulkarni et al., arXiv:2608.10157, 2026-08-10) → sbco-verifier-grounded-harness-optimization.md. Places the KB's self-improving-loop material in a **named research lineage** (Darwin Gödel Machine, Huxley Gödel Machine) and states the precondition the practitioner literature never does: self-*referential* self-improvement only works where "the competence required to perform the task coincides… with the competence required for self-modification, **which is the case for coding tasks**" — so the whole Ralph-loop/factory literature is quietly relying on coding being a special case. SBCO's alternative is self-*supervised* not self-referential: learn "a decomposed bank of **verifiers** and a harness policy via approximate block coordinate ascent… from its own graded feedback — with a fixed meta-agent and no human labels", matching a self-modifying baseline at **4–5.5× less compute**. This is LangChain's grader loop given a formal treatment, and the cheapest-known instance of maker≠checker. ## [2026-08-18] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (HEADLESS scheduled run, lookback 14d → since ~2026-08-04) — filed: 1 raw source (0 in-window; 1 deliberate out-of-window BACKFILL, ~10 weeks outside the window, flagged for Dannie's accept/reject). Effective new span **~1 day**, since both a headless run AND a full live-Chrome sweep ran on 08-17. Config loaded clean (topics 1–4 + 15 people current; the 08-17 recommendations to add eventmodelers.ai/docs/blog and nick-tune.me to watch-config have **not** yet been applied — the file is unchanged). CAPTURED (1) **out-of-window backfill, but the single most on-target coverage gap the KB has**: Martin Dilger, "Loop Engineering - Or Why You Should Never Argue With an Agent" (eventmodelers.ai blog, **2026-06-10**) → raw/articles/dilger-loop-engineering-never-argue-with-agent.md. Found by re-fetching the eventmodelers.ai `/docs/blog` index (the channel yesterday's run recommended adding permanently). **Why this closes a hole rather than padding raw/**: Topic-4 has now logged "**no authored primary from a tracked voice**" for *six consecutive sweeps* while the graph-vs-loop SEO tier crowded the searches — and this is exactly that: a tracked author, on Topic-4, published **2026-06-10**, i.e. *three days after* [[addyosmani-loop-engineering]] (Jun-07) and *two days before* [[swyx-loopcraft-art-of-stacking-loops]] (Jun-12). The KB captured both of those in the 06-28 batch and missed the Event-Modeling camp's contemporaneous entry into the same conversation. It is also the **only one of the three that lands the loop on Event Modeling**, so it sits on Topic-1 and Topic-4 simultaneously. CONTENT: attributes the origin to "Geoffrey Huntley's papers… the RALPH loop" and to his own prior practice ("wait. I'm already doing this. I just didn't have a word for it"). The method in one paragraph — define a task list, run tasks one at a time, **after each task record what you learned, then clear the context completely**; "The next iteration starts from scratch. No bias. No bad decisions lingering… Just the learnings - and the next task." The mechanism claim is sharper than the KB's existing primaries: hallucination is **not stochastic but structural** — "confusion is what produces hallucination. It's not a random event. It's the predictable result of a polluted context window" — and the common failure mode is prompting harder ("Just add another instruction in capitals. They are making things worse"). The title's rule: "Every agent will immediately tell you how right you are. That's not learning. That's hand-waving agreement… **The agent isn't learning from your corrections. It's just agreeing with you - and carrying the confusion forward.**" Hence: rule broken → no discussion → discard everything except the learnings → retry. THE LOAD-BEARING BRIDGE for the wiki: "**Event Modeling Is 'Loop Engineering' Applied** — The hard part in Loop Engineering is not implementing the loop, it's defining the list of tasks. In Event Modeling this happens naturally and is part of the process. Every 'slice' of functionality becomes a task." That is the [[loop-engineering]] ↔ [[vertical-slice-architecture]] ↔ [[event-modeling]] seam stated by a primary rather than inferred by us, and it is the *article-length* source behind the board mechanics the KB currently only holds as a LinkedIn note ([[dilger-event-modeling-agent-harness-24-7-loop]]): slice statuses (planned/assigned/blocked/in-progress/done), agents subscribing to `slice:changed`, claim-and-lock semantics, and the **three terminal outcomes including agent death** ("task stays in progress and times out after a while, another agent can pick up on it"). Two further points worth their own lines on ingest: (a) **"If something fails, the problem is in the spec, not the prompt"** — spec-as-debugging-surface, which is the constructive twin of [[ng-spec-driven-development-is-waterfall-in-markdown]]'s critique and pairs with [[dilger-is-code-still-the-source-of-truth]]; (b) an explicit **anti-overengineering rule for loops** — "The moment you start overengineering it - adding more state, more memory, more decision-making between iterations - you start reintroducing exactly the problems the loop was designed to eliminate… Simplicity isn't a limitation of loop engineering. It **is** loop engineering." That is a direct, named counter-position to the stacked-loops maximalism of [[swyx-loopcraft-art-of-stacking-loops]] and to the meta-harness layer in [[macmanus-schott-react-for-agents-flue-meta-harness]] — the KB currently has **no dissent** on record against loop-stacking, and this supplies it from inside the EM camp. Also connects backwards to the uncaptured Jan-2026 "RALPH Loop Explained" piece on the same blog (**NEW FLAG**: second Dilger loop primary, still missing). Touches [[loop-engineering]], [[event-modeling]], [[martin-dilger]], [[vertical-slice-architecture]], [[eventmodelers-ai]], [[spec-driven-development]], [[context-engineering]], [[agent-harness]], [[ralph-loop]], [[given-when-then]]. Ready to ingest if Dannie accepts the backfill; if not, delete the raw file. ## [2026-08-20] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (HEADLESS scheduled run, lookback 14d → since ~2026-08-06) — filed: 3 raw sources. CAPTURED: (1) Simon Willison, "Conceptual integrity and counting lines of code" (simonwillison.net, 2026-08-19) → raw/articles/willison-conceptual-integrity-and-counting-lines-of-code.md — highlights from his Talking Postgres appearance with Claire Giordano. Two arguments: (a) lines-of-code IS a meaningful agent-productivity signal because there was a hard human ceiling (~50–200 debugged lines/day; 200 = "an incredibly good day"), so 1000 lines/day of same-quality code is a real delta — but reaching it "takes a huge amount of skill and knowledge and experience"; and the new limiting factor is COGNITIVE CAPACITY, not code production ("I can churn out code a hundred times faster. I don't have the cognitive capacity to stay on top of 100 times the amount of code"), which is why you still need a team — to LOAD BALANCE cognitive capacity. (b) Brooks' CONCEPTUAL INTEGRITY (Mythical Man-Month) is what agents erode: cheap features mean "your software grows little weird bumps in funny different directions" → Claire Giordano's Winchester Mystery House analogy (140 rooms, kept adding forever). The mechanism named explicitly: discipline used to be enforced by COST OF TIME ("that would take me a week — I cannot justify that"); at one hour the filter is gone. Directly reinforces [[comprehension-debt]] / [[intent-debt]] and gives the KB a named design-integrity concept to hang the EM/spec-first argument on. (2) Rachel Laycock (CTO, Thoughtworks), "Citizens Build, Agents Execute, Experts Govern" (martinfowler.com/rachels-ramblings, 2026-08-19) → raw/articles/laycock-citizens-build-agents-execute-experts-govern.md — NEW AUTHOR/CHANNEL for the KB (Rachel's Ramblings launched ~Jul-2026 on martinfowler.com, announced in Fowler's Fragments 2026-08-18; not in watch-config's people list — candidate to ADD). Thesis: the gap isn't technology, it's what people think software ENGINEERING is; weekend-app "citizens" now build real software, agents execute, and the scarce resource is ENGINEERING JUDGEMENT ("Organisations don't run on code. They run on trust"). Reports from Thoughtworks' FOSE event: teams spend the day designing a SPECIFICATION, let agents work overnight, review next morning — and "when agents can generate lots of code very quickly, good design matters MORE, not less". Experts become "dramatically more leveraged": their job shifts to designing "the guardrails, the platforms, the engineering practices and the FEEDBACK LOOPS" — i.e. an executive-level restatement of [[harness-engineering]] / [[loop-engineering]], and a strong external corroboration of the KB's spec-first/[[event-modeling]]-as-agent-substrate line. (3) BACKFILL — Rachel Laycock, "The Conductor Developer" (same channel, 2026-07-31, just outside the window) → raw/articles/laycock-the-conductor-developer.md — the predecessor piece: she expected the bottleneck to migrate coding→design→architecture→verification and says "I was wrong" — it's HUMAN ATTENTION. Developers-as-conductors (agents = musicians; the conductor's value is "holding the whole system in their head"); reports practitioners running 8 parallel agents, "Ten. Twelve. Beyond that, they become the bottleneck" — an independent field number for Osmani's [[parallel-agent-limit]] / [[orchestration-tax]]; and argues the skills now needed are executive ones (manage energy not time, reduce unnecessary decisions, build systems that help your brain).
