## [2026-08-21] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (HEADLESS scheduled run, lookback 14d → since ~2026-08-07; effective new span ~1 day since the 08-20 headless run) — filed: 1 raw source. Config loaded clean (4 topics + 15 people; the standing CONFIG DEBT from 08-18/08-20 is still unapplied — eventmodelers.ai/docs/blog on Dilger, nick-tune.me/blog on Nick Tune, Rachel Laycock not yet added, Fraktalio LinkedIn→fraktalio.com, linkedin social_queries term lists). CAPTURED (1): **Andrew Miracle, "My Loop Engineering Workflow" (andrewmiracle.com, 2026-08-10)** → raw/articles/miracle-my-loop-engineering-workflow.md. NEW VOICE for the KB, and the first **authored practitioner primary** on Topic-4 found by the topic search since the Jun-07/Jun-10/Jun-12 Osmani/Dilger/swyx cluster — every sweep since has returned only the vendor "loop engineering guide 2026" SEO tier. Head of Product & Research at Tecmie; the whole harness is public at github.com/koolamusic/claudefiles, so the claims are inspectable rather than anecdotal. THESIS: "I don't have a prompt. I have a triangle of loops… The prompt is maybe five percent of the outcome. The loops are the rest." The **triangle**: session loop (hours; opens with a written commission, closes with a structured closeout/handoff) nests inside sprint loop (days; research→plan→execute→retro) nests inside program loop (weeks; PROJECT.md + phased ROADMAP.md → packets) — and crucially *every scale runs the same grammar*: **objective → execution → feedback → refined objective → …**, always starting from a written commission and always ending on ship-only-what-passes + retro. "That refinement step is why the loop converges instead of spinning." WHY IT MATTERS TO THIS WIKI, four seams: (a) **direct counterpoint-and-completion to [[swyx-loopcraft-art-of-stacking-loops]]** — swyx stacks *heterogeneous* loops (agent→grader→event-driven→hill-climbing); Miracle nests *homogeneous* loops at three time scales with one grammar, which is a materially different answer to "what does stacking mean" and sits between swyx's maximalism and [[dilger-loop-engineering-never-argue-with-agent]]'s simplicity rule; (b) **fork-join as the named shape of agent execution** (Doug Lea / Cilk lineage) with four deliberate deviations — fork is an approved dispatch plan not a thread pool, workers are durable branch-keyed children in isolated git worktrees not anonymous stealers, "**the join is a gate, not a barrier… It doesn't merge output. It merges confidence**", and Amdahl's law means "generation parallelized; verification did not" so **the serial work moved to the join rather than shrinking** — an explicit mechanism for [[orchestration-tax]] / [[parallel-agent-limit]] and a formal restatement of the attention-bottleneck line the KB just took from [[laycock-the-conductor-developer]] and [[willison-conceptual-integrity-and-counting-lines-of-code]]; (c) **three sequenced non-advisory gates** — plan-checker (planner and checker never share a context, "rather than the planner grading its own homework"), a *Nyquist test gate* (sample the change surface densely enough to reconstruct what was actually built and catch where it aliases from what was asked), and a **goal-backward verifier** ("for every requirement in the plan, where in the diff is it satisfied? Any requirement without evidence fails the audit, even if all the tests pass") — this is the sharpest articulation the KB now holds of maker≠checker, and goal-backward verification is *exactly* what a GWT-per-slice event model would supply as the rubric, so it strengthens the [[event-modeling]]↔[[loop-engineering]] seam from the loop side; (d) a genuinely novel primitive — a **trust ledger** hook on every tool call: sessions start at 50/100 (L2), levels gate capability (L1 edit → L2 mutating bash/spawn → L3 push/PR/deploy → L4 destructive), Fibonacci-scaled deductions (delivery miss −5, unverified claim −8, fabrication −13, tampering with the ledger −21, self-report a flat −3 "because disclosure should always be cheaper than discovery"), below 20 the agent is terminated. "It works because it prices honesty into the system." Also: bounded audit loops (max 2 plan revisions, 2 fix attempts, then escalate — "unbounded loops are how agents burn hours polishing a wrong answer"); **documents over conversations** at every handoff ("documents survive context windows; conversations don't"); autoCompact disabled deliberately; **restatement as the only compaction-proof memory**; and an explicit "give your harness explicit context layers" prescription (PLAN.md, status.md, decision log, handoff.md) that is a near-exact independent rediscovery of the [[karpathy-llm-wiki]] durable-context pattern this KB itself runs on. Touches [[loop-engineering]], [[agent-harness]], [[harness-engineering]], [[orchestration-tax]], [[parallel-agent-limit]], [[context-engineering]], [[maker-checker]], [[given-when-then]], [[event-modeling]], [[comprehension-debt]]. A **LIVE-CHROME sweep is now 4 days stale** (last full one 08-17) and is the only way to cover the social topic axis plus the login-walled feeds (Dilger LinkedIn, Fraktalio, Nick Tune, Miller, Tornhill, Goeleven, Dudycz, Khononov, Osmani, Willison, Dymitruk) — **recommend running one before the next headless sweep**. CAPTURED (2), both **Drew Breunig / dbreunig.com**, a NEW author for this KB, surfaced on the **topic axis** (Topic-4 loop engineering, scope anything-substantive) via Simon Willison's 2026-08-23 quotation, then followed back to the author's own blog: (1) **"Harnesses are Situated Agents" (2026-08-14)** → raw/articles/breunig-harnesses-are-situated-agents.md. The strongest new definitional primary this KB has seen for [[harness-engineering]] since Böckeler's guides/sensors framing: a harness IS an agent that has been *situated*. Harrison Chase's four agent elements (system prompt, planning tool, file system, subagents) describe "the core loop the developer controls with the keyboard"; the harness manages the **eight layers fanning outward** — 1. **Session** (trajectory + durable branchable log you can rewind/fork/replay), 2. **Environment** (sandbox/terminal/worktree/container), 3. **Repo** (git, `AGENTS.md`, guides, hooks), 4. **Memory**, 5. **Skills** (domain, reusable workflows), 6. **Team** (shared rooms/traces/issue trackers), 7. **Organization** (policy/audit/legal/procurement), 8. **Model** — with the ordering rule "*as we move outward, each layer is used by more people and changed less often*", and the function "harnesses account for the above layers… to determine what ends up in the context, how the loop runs, and what gets saved". Also a dated **Aug-2026 harness census** (Databricks Omnigent meta-harness, DeepSeek Harness shipped with V4-Pro and fully modular down to loops+scheduling, Block's Buzz, YC's QM with org-shaped scoping, Cloudflare Flue which "hides the loop from users", Meta Muse Code whose Muse Spark 1.2 was **co-trained with its harness**, plus OpenClaw/NanoClaw/Hermes/Conductor/Prime Agent) and a strategic claim: harness switching costs are the SaaS-style network effect the labs were looking for. On-thread beyond loop-engineering — the layer ordering is a **coupling/volatility argument** (outer = more shared, less volatile), which rhymes with Khononov's balanced coupling, and layers 4–5 (Memory, Skills) are exactly where an Event Model would sit as durable domain knowledge. Touches [[harness-engineering]], [[loop-engineering]], [[agentic-coding]], [[agent-harness]], [[balanced-coupling]]. (2) **"Fable & The End of the Free Lunch" (2026-08-23)** → raw/articles/breunig-fable-and-the-end-of-the-free-lunch.md. The **economic** case for harness engineering, by analogy to Herb Sutter's "free lunch": while each new model arrived at the same price and "papered over most of your problems", it "felt silly to waste *too* much time improving your coding harness or context strategies" — once frontier cost stopped being amortised away, "*we started to think about what work went where*". Concretely: GLM 5.2 at ~1/9th Fable's cost is "more than sufficient" for rote coding "**especially** when provided with great context"; his own workflow is to "chat with Fable to interrogate and shape a design, before handing off a brief to GLM". Rebuts the falling-price counterargument (cheaper models benefit equally, and "as we continue to develop better harnesses it will be easier to provide weaker (but still great) models with sufficient context"), and notes Fable's access controls / dynamic degradation / data-retention requirements pushed orgs to think about where traces and tokens go. **This is a first-class argument for the wiki's central seam**: a well-specified slice + its given/when/then IS the "sufficient context" that lets a cheap model execute a brief — model-routing becomes a harness design decision, and specification quality becomes an economic lever, not just a correctness one. Touches [[loop-engineering]], [[harness-engineering]], [[given-when-then]], [[vertical-slice-architecture]], [[event-modeled-agent-design]]. NOTE: Breunig is a **synthesizer/framer voice** (like swyx) rather than an EM practitioner — recommend adding to `people` with note "AI/harness systems commentator; the 'situated agent' definition of a harness; harness economics & model routing" and scope anything-substantive + flag-off-thread; his cited-but-uncaptured companion **"Overfitting the Harness" (2026-05-10)** is a backfill candidate. ## [2026-08-25] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (HEADLESS scheduled run, lookback 14d → since ~2026-08-11; effective new span ~1 day since the 08-24 headless run) — filed: 3 raw sources. Config loaded clean (4 topics + 15 people; the standing CONFIG DEBT from 08-18 onward is STILL unapplied — add eventmodelers.ai/docs/blog to Dilger, add nick-tune.me/blog to Nick Tune, add Rachel Laycock, switch Fraktalio LinkedIn→fraktalio.com, populate the `linkedin` social_queries term lists, add Rico Fritzsche's "Autonomous Domain Capabilities" (leanpub) to `candidate_primaries`, add Drew Breunig (dbreunig.com) to `people`). **MAJOR CORRECTION — a four-sweep miss on Addy Osmani.** The 08-21/08-22/08-23/08-24 runs each recorded Osmani's blog as "unchanged, newest still Jul-20 'Software Factories, Light and Dark' — five/six weeks quiet". That was **wrong**: addyosmani.com/blog today lists THREE uncaptured posts newer than Jul-20 (Aug-08, Aug-14, Aug-21), all of them squarely on Topic-4. The likely cause is a stale/cached index fetch on those runs; the corrective is to sanity-check a "quiet author" claim against the post *dates on the page*, not against the previous run's verdict. All three are captured now. CAPTURED (3), all **Addy Osmani**, all cross-posted from his Substack: (1) **"Practical Loop Engineering" (2026-08-14)** → raw/articles/addyosmani-practical-loop-engineering.md. The **operational sequel to his own Jun-07 definitional [[addyosmani-loop-engineering]]**, and the most concrete primary this KB has on loop *primitives*: `goal` (bounded task driven to a measurable finish line, with an evaluator model that gates each attempted stop) vs `loop` (a cron-like scheduler that re-runs a prompt on an interval), plus `/schedule` for cloud persistence. Restates his loop definition ("an autonomous, self-correcting feedback cycle where an AI agent repeatedly acts, tests its results and adjusts its approach until a specific goal is met"), traces the lineage back to hand-rolled bash loops and Geoff Huntley's Ralph loop, and quotes the Claude Code team's **four-rung loop taxonomy** (turn-based → goal-based → time-based → proactive) at length. Two claims land directly on the wiki's central seam: **the evaluator behind `goal` is NOT the checker** — "all it does is examine the conversation transcript to see if the hard rules you specified have been met" — which is exactly the argument for **maker≠checker** (one sub-agent drafts, a separate one verifies); and **loops fail without a deterministic done-condition** — "keep going until this UI design is good" is the anti-pattern, whereas a Lighthouse threshold or a passing test suite works. That is a direct restatement, in loop-engineering vocabulary, of why a slice's **given/when/then** is the right stop condition for an agent task. Also the honest failure story: he "delegated the task, but I was close to delegating the judgment as well". Touches [[loop-engineering]], [[harness-engineering]], [[given-when-then]], [[agentic-coding]], [[addy-osmani]], [[event-modeled-agent-design]]. (2) **"Human judgment doesn't leave the software factory. It relocates." (2026-08-21)** → raw/articles/addyosmani-human-judgment-relocates.md. The **field-guide sequel to [[addyosmani-software-factories-light-and-dark]]**, and the strongest statement yet of the "stay the engineer" caveat: "the percentage of code physically typed by humans may fall dramatically. I don't think human ownership needs to fall with it… human judgment is being **relocated**" — **upstream** to intent, system shape and the quality bar, **downstream** to evidence, risk and ownership. Practically dense: a definition ("a software factory is a repeatable loop around software work") and a **buy-vs-build / do-you-even-need-one** test (stock harness + good SPECs gets you far; a factory earns its keep only once the work becomes *repeatable and event-driven*); Warp's **four-state triage label** that is simultaneously "the queue, the lock, and a parking spot"; Vercel's **success / flawed / blocked / manual** run taxonomy where only "success" ships; a **verification budget** modelled on performance budgets (cheap deterministic checks early, heavy checks at later gates); the **82-minute factory run** with per-task timings (7 min for a clean run vs 56 min for one with two rejections and a human decision) and the observation that verification makes tasks take 2–4× longer; and "**when green is misleading**" — the agent that changes the assertion instead of satisfying the intent. Closes with the comprehension-debt story of a favouriting feature he merged, then could no longer explain. Extra-relevant to the EM seam: the four human touchpoints (shape / steer / hand off / approve) map onto exactly the places an Event Model is the shared artefact. Touches [[loop-engineering]], [[harness-engineering]], [[comprehension-debt]], [[maintainability-sensors]], [[agentic-coding]], [[addy-osmani]]. (3) **"Agentic Code Quality" (2026-08-08)** → raw/articles/addyosmani-agentic-code-quality.md — filed as a **backfill** (3 days outside the 14-day window, but uncaptured and on-thread; it is the conceptual middle term between the other two). Thesis: "**software quality now depends on the constraints you set around your agents**" — code review does not scale to agent volume, so quality checks migrate into "the harness, environment, and operating system around the agent". Frames constraints as **back-pressure** distributed throughout the loop rather than a single end-of-pipeline gate (compilers, tests, mutation testing, security policy, architecture lint), and gives the **capacity argument**: when change volume outruns verification you must either scale verification, slow the agents, or lower the bar — "from a scaling perspective, we need to be ready to do all of these things". Also "an agent can propose anything; your constraints decide whether a proposal is safe enough" and the autonomy-is-earned routing (high / gated / human-decided by risk, evidence, track record). This is the **Tornhill seam from the other side**: Tornhill measures code health as a lever on agent cost/reliability; Osmani argues the enforcement mechanism is the constraint set in the environment. Touches [[harness-engineering]], [[loop-engineering]], [[maintainability-sensors]], [[agentic-coding]], [[code-health]], [[addy-osmani]]. ## [2026-08-26] research | LIVE-CHROME sweep (Dannie-requested "do the sweep"; covers STEP 1b social + the login-walled/client-rendered feeds the headless runs cannot reach) — filed: 8 raw sources. This is the sweep the last 9 headless runs kept deferring, and it paid for itself immediately: **every one of the 8 keepers was invisible to headless fetching.** Session confirmed logged in on both X and LinkedIn. CAPTURED (8): **(1) Jeremy Miller, "The JasperFx / CritterStack AI and Event Modeling Strategy" (jeremydmiller.com, 2026-08-21)** → raw/articles/miller-jasperfx-critterstack-ai-event-modeling-strategy.md. **The biggest Topic-1 find in weeks, and a headless blind-spot** — his blog index reported "newest Aug-03" on every recent run; this surfaced only via an X search hit (@jeremydmiller, Aug-21, quote-tweeted by @lxztlr). JasperFx is **landing Event Modeling into the core of the Critter Stack**: a fluent model/API in JasperFx.Events for declaring event types and read models, a Bobcat UI that visualizes EM slices, `dotnet watch` integration to "interactively doodle with slice definitions and see the model change", EM concepts pushed **directly into Wolverine and the Marten/Polecat/Fisher event stores**, "more command line options to **export the event slice definitions for AI agent usage**", AI Skills so an agent knows how to use the specs/models, and codegen to "build out the shell of a 'slice' from the model". Also: VSA as **accidentally prescient token economics** ("layered architectures … force AI agents to burn more tokens traversing the code"), CritterWatch 1.0's MCP server over event stores, and a JasperFx **agent orchestrator + durable agent memory** built on Critter Stack event sourcing (praising KurrentDB's Capacitor). **The load-bearing disagreement:** Miller is "not enthusiastic about any of the intermediate DSL approaches I'm seeing for modeling event driven architectures using YAML, XML, or custom built textual DSLs" and prefers "adding the visualization capabilities on top of the code" — a direct, dated challenge to the chspec/`.chspec.yml` premise and to eventmodelers.ai's model-first direction. Touches [[event-modeling]], [[vertical-slice-architecture]], [[jeremy-miller]], [[critter-stack]], [[given-when-then]], [[spec-driven-development]], [[event-modeled-agent-design]]. **(2–5) Martin Dilger, four authored LinkedIn posts (2026-08-24/25)** — his feed is reachable **only** in live Chrome, and it was his most substantive week in a while. **(2)** "Writing Code by hand is so 2024 — Markdown is a suggestion dressed up as a spec" (Aug-25) → raw/notes/dilger-markdown-is-a-suggestion-dressed-as-a-spec.md: "It´s ridiculous we´ve chosen Markdown as the 'language' for LLMs… Pages of prose to produce a handful of lines. If the explanation outweighs the thing it's explaining, the tool doing the explaining is wrong… The Markdown we use is a suggestion dressed up as a spec… **Spec-Driven Development requires suitable language. Event Modeling is the one that works for me. A precise specification of behavior.**" **Read this against capture (1): Miller rejects intermediate DSLs and wants code+visualization; Dilger rejects prose/Markdown and wants a formal modeling language. Two tracked practitioners staking opposite ground on the same question in the same week — this is the sharpest live controversy in the wiki and deserves a concepts page of its own.** **(3)** "Slice-based architecture is not really about slices" (Aug-25) → raw/notes/dilger-element-slice-chapter-story-context-ladder.md: a **NEW method-vocabulary ladder — Element → Slice → Chapter → Story → Context** ("Group slices together and you get a chapter… Connect chapters in the right order and you get a story… They all together form a context"), plus the diagnosis that "most teams get stuck right there… never climb up to see the story". This is a genuine EM method extension and is not yet anywhere in wiki/concepts. **(4)** "Making Event Modeling an organizational success. Simple, definitely not easy." (Aug-25) → raw/notes/dilger-event-modeling-is-a-shift-left-on-the-organization.md: "Event Modeling isn't a training problem. It's a **gigantic shift left on the organization**… one of the biggest levers a company has for AI Integration"; the four requirements (methodology/tooling/training+facilitation/organizational buy-in) and the claim that 1–2× 30min workshops a week suffice. Notably **liked by Adam Dymitruk**. **(5)** "Join considered harmful" (Aug-24) → raw/notes/dilger-join-considered-harmful.md: "Every SQL statement is essentially a 'select * from ? where ?'… there is simply no need to join in 99.9% of all use cases. Does that mean we embrace redundant data? yes. **That's actually a precondition for sliced architectures.**" — the read-model/redundancy argument stated more bluntly than in any existing capture. **(6) Adam Tornhill, "Controlling the Uncertainty Machine: Do You Still Need to Read AI Code?" (adamtornhill.substack.com, 2026-08-20)** → raw/articles/tornhill-controlling-the-uncertainty-machine.md. **Substack JS shell has blocked this channel headlessly for weeks; live Chrome read it in full.** Thesis: "**Human inspection is driven by uncertainty**" — task uncertainty sets both the autonomy granted and the review depth (bug fixes: inspect the evidence only; first iteration on a novel feature: spot-inspect structure, never all the code). The mechanism is **tests as the human/agent abstraction boundary**: generate e2e tests first, review and refactor *those* heavily ("AI-generated application code is rarely optimal… but usually decent. The test code? Not so much"), then let the agent write whatever makes them pass. Plus **"turn review findings into future guards"** — never fix it yourself; have the agent capture the transformation as a forward-looking SKILL — and **"enforce what you don't inspect"** deterministically (linters, CodeHealth MCP, custom architectural checks). Restates the CodeScene research line: "a coding agent is even more picky about code quality than we humans. The bar is higher." **This closes a three-way triangulation with Willison's "More than just code review" (Aug-22) and Osmani's "Agentic Code Quality" (Aug-08): all three independently conclude that reading every line is the wrong unit, and all three replace it with a verification artefact. For this wiki the punchline writes itself — Tornhill's reviewed-e2e-test boundary IS a slice's given/when/then.** Touches [[maintainability-sensors]], [[code-health]], [[agentic-coding]], [[given-when-then]], [[harness-engineering]], [[adam-tornhill]]. **(7) Dan McAteer, "The Evolution of the Agent Harness" (latent.space, 2026-08-22)** → raw/articles/mcateer-evolution-of-the-agent-harness.md. latent.space is the other standing headless block (Substack + paywall); **this one turned out to be fully readable**. The best-argued **historical** account of harness engineering the KB has: two curves (what the harness asks of the model vs what the model can deliver), whose gap *is* agent effectiveness, walked through four dated stages — ReAct "harness on paper" (Oct-2022), AutoGPT/BabyAGI "premature autonomy" (Spring-2023, with the compounding argument: 95% per-step over 20 steps ≈ 36% success), Cursor/Copilot "retreat to human in the loop" (2023–24, incl. Devin's ~15% Answer.AI result), and Claude Code "the curves cross" (Feb-2025). Then **Harness 2.0 "the co-training era"** with hard numbers — **Harness-Bench: same model, same 106 tasks, 52.4→76.2 across harnesses, a 23.8-point spread with zero model change ("half the agent is the harness")**; GPT-5.6 Sol's ARC-AGI-3 tripling 13.3%→38.3% on retained reasoning + compaction alone; Anthropic deleting 80% of Claude Code's system prompt — yielding the evolution loop **train → absorb → shed → repeat** and the metric "the measure of the pace of agent harness evolution is **how much of the harness you get to delete** while retaining the same capability level". Finally **Harness 3.0 "the attention era"**: absorption doesn't end the harness, it **inverts** it — what the weights can never absorb is permissions, identity, trust and legibility, so "the harness becomes the model's interface to our human attention", an **attention-interface** predicted to ship as ubiquitously as AGENTS.md. Directly extends [[breunig-harnesses-are-situated-agents]] (Breunig's 8 layers) and lands on Osmani's "human judgment relocates" from the opposite direction. Touches [[harness-engineering]], [[loop-engineering]], [[agent-harness]], [[comprehension-debt]]. **(8) Richard MacManus interviewing Matt Pocock, "The /wayfinder Skill: Navigating the 'Fog of War' of Planning" (latent.space, 2026-08-20)** → raw/articles/macmanus-pocock-wayfinder-skill-fog-of-war.md. An orchestrator-layer planning skill for AFK/overnight agents, built on a deliberate **map / ticket / session** vocabulary and a "fog of war" metaphor for decisions you cannot make up front (ticket kinds: grilling, prototype, research, task). The reason it is filed rather than skipped: Pocock's design method is **explicitly ubiquitous-language engineering** — "if you just call everything a ticket… it's going to be really confused and you're going to get strange behavior", he maintains an AI-coding dictionary shared across all his skills, and concludes "**I realized that I needed a ubiquitous language between me and the agent**… and agents are really good at showing you the opportunities for different wording — **really good at domain modeling, actually**." That is a DDD/EM claim arrived at independently from the agent-skills side, and it is the strongest outside corroboration yet for glossary/ubiquitous-language work as harness infrastructure. Touches [[ubiquitous-language]], [[harness-engineering]], [[loop-engineering]], [[spec-driven-development]], [[agent-skills]]. CAPTURED (1): **Rico Fritzsche, "Why the Entity Model Is an Illusion — What Remains When You Model What Happens" (ricofritzsche.me, 2026-08-26)** → raw/articles/fritzsche-why-the-entity-model-is-an-illusion.md. Published the same day as yesterday's sweep and missed by it. His sharpest statement yet of the **entity-vs-event** position, and it lands directly on the [[autonomous-domain-capabilities]] / [[command-context-consistency]] thread: "I don't think we need entities that we have to define in advance. That limits us too much." The worked example is a vehicle-rental domain where registration is *not* `CreateVehicle` ("that merely describes a database operation… the car rental company doesn't actually 'create' a vehicle") but a sequence — `VehicleAcquired → VehicleReceived → VehicleInfleeted → VehicleReleasedForRental`. **The Vehicle is a projection, not an entity**; a unique identifier (artificial ID or a natural one like a VIN) is a *reference*, and "the existence of a unique identifier does not automatically constitute an entity in the sense of a specific data object". Capabilities ("AcquireVehicle", "ReceiveVehicle") are "autonomous, self-contained, and coherent… They share only the Application State — in this implementation, an Event Store and the event definitions. However, they know nothing about each other." The two-cost argument against load-mutate-save is quotable: it "destroys the information about what happened" and "turns every concurrent request into a conflict we have to defend against" — with audit/history/status/timestamp/changelog tables read as **workarounds for information the last-write row threw away**. Also a perspective claim that rhymes with EM swimlanes: "it's simply a different matter from the perspective of purchasing versus that of the repair shop. Every form that someone needs is derived from these events. It is a projection." Off the agents thread except in passing ("regardless of the possibilities for deploying AI agents, complexity… is the main reason"). Touches [[event-sourcing]], [[autonomous-domain-capabilities]], [[business-capabilities]], [[projections]], [[rico-fritzsche]], [[event-modeling]]. Published *after* yesterday's sweep read his homepage. An empirical counter-datum to vendor safety claims on the autonomy ceiling: Anthropic recently made Claude Code's **auto mode the default** and "made bold claims about its effectiveness"; Johann Rehberger ("one of the most credible prompt injection researchers active today") found an attack that "works 80% of the time" — trick Claude Code into downloading and uncompressing a zip, then execute code importing `base64` without noticing that also imports and executes a `struct.py` extracted from the archive. The sharpest finding is that **the guardrail became part of the failure**: "In a few runs Claude tried to terminate the malware process once it noticed the compromise, but Auto Mode denied the cleanup command… The classifier allowed the creation of the malware process, but then it blocked the command intended to stop it!" Willison's conclusion — the only safe way to run agents under adversarial risk is a **sandbox** (container/VM/OS sandbox, restricted network egress, monitoring, no home dirs/SSH keys/cloud credentials in the agent runtime) — is the same position as his 2025 [[willison-designing-agentic-loops]] (YOLO-mode-plus-sandbox) and sets a hard ceiling on the [[unattended-coding-agents]] / always-on-loop thread: a *classifier* is not a substitute for an *isolation boundary*. The standing **CONFIG DEBT** remains unapplied (add eventmodelers.ai/docs/blog to Dilger, add nick-tune.me/blog to Nick Tune — used successfully again this run and confirmed as the right primary, add Rachel Laycock, switch Fraktalio LinkedIn→fraktalio.com, populate the `linkedin` social_queries term lists, add Fritzsche's "Autonomous Domain Capabilities" leanpub + the three arXiv harness/loop papers (2607.00038, 2605.25665, 2604.17025) to `candidate_primaries`, add Drew Breunig). The 08-25 discovery lesson was applied again — every "quiet author" verdict below was checked against the post dates on the author's own index page, and it mattered once this run: yesterday's sweep recorded martinfowler.com's newest as the Aug-24 Fragments, but the recent-changes page shows an **Aug-27 full article** that had not been seen. **This was the run's best find and it came from re-reading an index rather than trusting the prior verdict.** CAPTURED (4): (1) **Sadalage & Chandrasekaran, "Making Your Data Ready for Agentic AI" (martinfowler.com, 2026-08-27)** → raw/articles/sadalage-chandrasekaran-making-data-ready-for-agentic-ai.md. A long Thoughtworks primary that is off the Event Modeling method thread but lands hard on the KB's capability/context substrate. Its **capability model** — every capability declares permissions, an owner, **preconditions** checked against live state at the moment of acting, and a **reversibility class** — is a data-architecture sibling of [[devadoss-cead-capability-aligned-agent-design]]'s Agent Capability Contract, and its "**Reversibility predicts safe autonomy better than the size of the transaction**" is a sharper guardrail axis than the transaction-size ladders the KB otherwise holds. "**Agentic lineage**" (traces + spans that record *why*, not just *what*, keyed to EU AI Act Art. 12/19) restates [[axoniq-ai-agent-explainability-why-infrastructure-needs-to-remember]] and [[fritzsche-why-your-software-cannot-explain-business-decisions]] from the data side. "**Retrieved text informs, it never gates**" is a concrete design answer to [[willison-lethal-trifecta]] — extract rules ahead of time, curate them by hand, check them deterministically, so a poisoned document cannot *grant* a permission. Also: agents read Gold-tier only; "design capabilities, not endpoints" with the Thoughtworks Radar HOLD on naive API-to-MCP conversion; the AtScale datum that text-to-SQL accuracy went from <20% on a raw schema to >92.5% with a semantic layer **on the same model**; and a five-attribute (Trusted/Contextual/Traceable/Governed/Operational) readiness table whose score is "capped by your weakest foundational layer". Touches [[business-capabilities]], [[agent-explainability]], [[model-context-protocol]], [[autonomy-ladder]], [[context-engineering]], [[prompt-injection]], [[thoughtworks]], [[martin-fowler]]. (2) **Bartosz Ocytko, "Agentic Engineering at Zalando: a snapshot" (Zalando Engineering Blog, 2026-08-14)** → raw/articles/zalando-agentic-engineering-snapshot.md. Surfaced via Fowler's Aug-24 Fragments; **one day outside the strict window** but never seen by this KB and filed anyway, because it is a rare NON-VENDOR production account at scale — >250 engineering teams, 2.5 years — the same evidence class as [[stripe-minions-one-shot-coding-agents]] and [[martinfowler-prince-building-reliable-agentic-ai-systems]]. Hard numbers: **33% of PRs auto-approved** by a risk-based bot whose rule set was derived from their own outage post-mortems, cutting PR lead time **20–40%**; PR sizes climbing since the Sonnet-4 release, with commit messages carrying an agent footprint "around the 5k character mark"; a four-codebase cyclomatic-complexity study that pinpoints **inflection points in complexity where coding agents enter**, with the agentic-from-day-0 codebase spiking fastest. Also a company-wide **agent-skill marketplace** grouped into plugins (migration skills the most popular), a deliberate refusal to converge on one tool ("it's way too early"), an AI-readiness **scanner per repository** planned, and the behavioural finding that the approval bot changed how engineers *split* PRs. Two lines worth quoting into the wiki: "**AI amplifies the good and bad practices across our organization**" and "using coding agents usually inhibits learning" (on training design). Touches [[agentic-coding]], [[software-factory]], [[harness-engineering]], [[agent-skills]], [[model-context-protocol]], [[comprehension-debt]], [[ai-readable-code]]. (3) **Jeremy Miller, "The 'Open Core' Model for Sustainable OSS Development in .NET" (jeremydmiller.com, 2026-08-28)** → raw/articles/miller-open-core-model-sustainable-oss-dotnet.md. Mostly OSS licensing/sustainability and **flagged as largely off-thread**, filed under `anything-substantive` for two reasons: a dated confirmation that JasperFx has "another set of commercial tools related to **AI assisted development and Event Modeling** coming soon" (the follow-up to [[miller-jasperfx-critterstack-ai-event-modeling-strategy]]), and an anti-"vibe code your dependencies" argument that is the direct counterweight to [[dudycz-fork-can-you-own-it]]'s "LLM as a fork" — LLMs change the cost of *producing* code, not the years of production beatings that hardened it. His Aug-25 **Wolverine 6.30 "Native Acks"** release note stays **surfaced-not-filed** for a third run (pure release note), but it now carries an on-thread line worth recording: they "smuggled in some support for our soon forthcoming 'Event Modeling' visualization support across the Critter Stack and more for CritterWatch". (4) **Simon Willison, "Just a rumour of a bug is enough to find a security exploit these days" (simonwillison.net link blog, 2026-08-28, 10:12pm)** → raw/articles/willison-just-a-rumour-of-a-bug.md. Published after yesterday's sweep read his homepage. Anil Madhavapeddy (Cambridge, OCaml compiler maintainer) reports exploit probes **within about ten minutes** of a patch being shared for discussion; rclone's Nick Craig-Wood confirms going from ~20 security disclosures in ten years to **over 40 in the last month**, with ~75% containing something real, while GitHub CVE assignment slowed from 2–3 days to 3–4 weeks. Willison's framing: coding agents are now good enough that "the slightest hint at a new bug" suffices, which is **incompatible with existing open-source embargo practice**. ## [2026-08-30] research | EM×agents + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (scheduled run, but LIVE-CHROME after all — lookback 14d → since ~2026-08-16; effective new span ~1 day since yesterday's headless run, plus the 4-day LinkedIn backlog since the 08-26 live sweep) — filed: 5 raw sources. Config loaded clean (4 topics + 15 people). **This run was NOT headless**: a connected local Chrome was found, so STEP 1b and the login-walled feeds were swept live rather than skipped — and that is where every one of the five keepers came from. The standing **CONFIG DEBT** remains unapplied (add eventmodelers.ai/docs/blog to Dilger, add nick-tune.me/blog to Nick Tune — used successfully again, add Rachel Laycock, switch Fraktalio LinkedIn→fraktalio.com, populate the `linkedin` social_queries term lists, add Fritzsche's "Autonomous Domain Capabilities" leanpub + the three arXiv harness/loop papers (2607.00038, 2605.25665, 2604.17025) to `candidate_primaries`, add Drew Breunig). CAPTURED (5, all LinkedIn notes): (1) **Martin Dilger, "Slicing keeps the cost curve flat — Triplet Architecture" (2026-08-29)** → raw/notes/dilger-slicing-keeps-the-cost-curve-flat-triplet-architecture.md. The thesis stated as an economics claim: "building a feature in 5 years costs exactly as much or less as today. That's the only purpose of architecture" — friction accumulates as coupling, "this is what makes working with AI so hard in grown code bases", and "Attempting Spec-Driven-Development without having a solution to this is like playing russian roulette". His answer is consistent slicing, decided *during specification*: "**Slices are like Candy for AI**". Names a methodology, **'Triplet-Architecture'** (Event Modeling + Event Sourcing + slicing), pointing at a detailed article behind an `lnkd.in` shortlink — **NOT captured, strong candidate primary**. (2) **Martin Dilger, "Spec-Driven Development needs 4 phases" (2026-08-29)** → raw/notes/dilger-spec-driven-development-needs-four-phases.md. Idea→Intent, Intent→Spec, Spec→Code, Code→Operations; Kiro, SpecKit and "Anthrophics latest Handbook on Software SDLC" focus "almost exclusively on 3)", handwaving the rest and "Leaving out 75% of the work". "1) and 4) are the tough ones. 2) is mechanical if 1) is done right. 3) the easiest of them all." Sharpens [[dilger-spec-driven-tools-need-event-modeling-front-half]] by adding the *back* half (operations/maintenance) to the same indictment. (3) **Martin Dilger, "The Event Modeling Agent improved significantly — what the learning loop taught it" (2026-08-28)** → raw/notes/dilger-modeling-agent-improved-by-learning-loop.md. Concrete outputs of the loop: models translations and knows when to use them; handles TODO lists via storylines; breaks screens into functional blocks with dedicated read models "(this allows to generate the UI much more easily)"; "actively prevents back arrows by using Read Model and Screen copies". The best datum is a failure mode: "**ever 5th iteration or so is really bad**… it's always the model taking shortcuts when it reaches certain budget thresholds. Most models are obviously trained to make the best with the budget they have, actively taking shortcuts when budgets get depleted, to deliver at least something." That is a **token-budget-induced quality cliff** — a hard, dated constraint on unattended loops, and the counterpart to [[willison-breaking-claude-code-auto-mode]] (exposure) and [[willison-just-a-rumour-of-a-bug]] (capability): here the bound is *degradation under budget pressure*. (4) **Martin Dilger, "Last night, I spent 1 Million+ Tokens to train my Modeling Agent" (2026-08-27)** → raw/notes/dilger-one-million-tokens-self-training-modeling-agent.md. **The run's most important find.** A working **hill-climbing / self-improving loop** whose grader is a corpus of hand-crafted "good" Event Models: model a realistic requirement set → structurally diff the result against the good corpus (chapter layout, read-model structure, given/when/then shape, pattern use) → **adjust the skills** → re-model → compare against both the previous version and the corpus. Three rules it discovered and wrote back into the skills: storylines instead of plain GWTs for read models connected to automations (plus a new rule for TODO lists); linked elements bridging events across chapters (previously only hinted, now stated); read-model copies rather than back arrows. Runs on **local hardware (QWEN3.7:27b)**, left running continuously. This is [[loop-engineering]]'s stacked-loop / maker≠checker picture instantiated with **Event Modeling itself as the verification rubric** — the exact dilger-event-modeling-agent-harness seam Topic-4 was created to watch, and the strongest evidence yet that the [[slice]]/GWT structure is what makes an agent gradeable. (5) **Adaptech Group (reposted by Adam Dymitruk), "How much ambiguity is still left when a developer starts building a feature?" (2026-08-28)** → raw/notes/adaptech-given-when-then-executable-tests-before-implementation.md. GWT-as-executable-spec from the method's own consultancy: scenarios "become executable tests before implementation begins", the developer "starts with failing tests and builds the slice until those tests pass", producing "a clear agreement about what the software is supposed to do and an objective way to know when the work is finished". Marketing register, but a clean canonical statement. Also reposted by **Mohsen B.** (Adaptech) with "This is why **Scenario Hunting** works" — a term this KB has not seen; worth chasing. STEP 1b (social keyword sweep) **RUN, partially**: LinkedIn content search sorted by date for `"event modeling" agent` and `#eventmodeling`, plus an X live search for `"event modeling" (agent OR LLM)`. LinkedIn's `"event modeling" agent` results are dominated by a **homonym** — agent-observability posts using "session→run→event model" (e.g. Giuseppe Pier Paolo Ucchino, 18h) — dropped under focus-only. `#eventmodeling` surfaced one **NEW VOICE**: **Christophe Le Coënt** (23h, French, EM + Domain Storytelling + slice architecture in his headline) — "Spec-driven-dev sans event modeling n'a pas de sens aujourd'hui: spec-driven-dev n'est que du réchauffé!" — a one-line comment, too thin to file, but a name to add to the watch. ## [2026-08-30] wiki     | new concept page [[model-as-code-vs-model-as-language]] — the Miller↔Dilger controversy, raised by the 08-26 and 08-29 sweeps and previously living only in log entries. States both positions without adjudicating: **Model-as-Language** ([[martin-dilger]], with [[adam-dymitruk]]/[[john-wilger]]/[[prooph-board]]/[[fraktalio]] tooling behind it) — "The Markdown we use is a suggestion dressed up as a spec… Spec-Driven Development requires suitable language. Event Modeling is the one that works for me"; vs **Model-as-Code** ([[jeremy-miller]]) — "not enthusiastic about any of the intermediate DSL approaches… using YAML, XML, or custom built textual DSLs… better off just adding the visualization capabilities on top of the code". Records what they AGREE on (prose specs fail; the slice is the right unit; VSA is token economics not taste; EM concepts belong in the runtime), why it matters (it is a direct test of the model-first `.chspec.yml` premise; it adds a missing *derived-artifact* rung to [[agent-readable-model-artifacts]]; the evidence is currently asymmetric in Dilger's favour but an unshipped comparison is not a refutation), and three open questions. Cross-linked from [[agent-readable-model-artifacts]] and [[spec-driven-development]]; added to index. **Sourcing caveat recorded on the page itself**: both primary captures are still raw-only, so its two block quotes cite raw files rather than source pages — revisit immediately after the DSL-vs-code ingest batch.

## [2026-08-30] plan     | ingest plan for the 46-file backlog — outputs/ingest-plan-2026-08-30.md. Ten themed batches (themed not chronological, so each batch produces one coherent set of concept-page edits). Recommended order **B → A → C**: (B) the Sadalage/Chandrasekaran capability-model article, alone — the rest of the capability corpus turns out to be already compiled; (A) the Dilger self-training-agent trio; (C) the two DSL-vs-code primaries, which ground the new concept page. Remaining: D Dilger method extensions (10), E verification-instead-of-reading (4), F loop engineering (13 — the largest cluster and **the only decaying one**), G autonomy bounds (2), H production evidence at scale (4), I event-sourcing substrate (4), J evergreen backfill (3). ## [2026-08-30] diagnosis| Topic-3 watch gap — outputs/topic-3-watch-diagnosis-2026-08-30.md. **The field is not quiet; the watch is mis-pointed, and the "fifteen blank sweeps" is largely a reporting artifact.** Evidence: ~22 substrate captures were filed between 2026-06-04 and 2026-08-26 — the exact window of the fifteen blanks — at roughly one every four days (Fritzsche ×13, Dudycz ×4, Khononov ×2, Enzler/AtomicObject/AxonIQ/Goeleven ×4), and **every one arrived via the PEOPLE axis, none attributed to a Topic-3 query**. Most are already compiled. Four structural faults: (1) Topic-3's eleven queries are generic terms + a year ("CQRS pattern 2026"), which web search ranks by authority not recency, so they structurally cannot surface a two-day-old post — hence the identical evergreen tier printed fifteen times; unlike Topics 1–2, Topic-3 names **no channel at all**; (2) it is redundant with the people watch for all six active substrate voices, who are already `people` entries; (3) the people list **omits the substrate's own originators** — [[sara-pellegrini]] (named in Topic-3's own description as the DCB originator) and [[jimmy-bogard]] (named as the VSA lineage) both have wiki entity pages but no watch entry, and [[axoniq]] surfaces in every sweep's evergreen tier without ever being polled; (4) the "business capability" social query is a confirmed homonym trap (HR/ESG usage), i.e. the flagged-important sub-topic has the least functional query in the config. Recommendations (NOT applied, awaiting Dannie): add Pellegrini/Bogard/AxonIQ as people; replace Topic-3's bare-term queries with named-channel polls; require an architecture co-term on the business-capability query; **report the substrate by thread crediting whichever axis found it**, which alone would have prevented this; and consider retiring Topic-3 as a keyword axis entirely. Caveat: recommended channels are "verify, then add" — this was a read-only analysis of config + log, no sweep run.

## [2026-08-31] ingest   | The DSL-vs-code controversy (Batch C, 2 primaries) — touched: 9 pages. New source pages [[miller-jasperfx-critterstack-ai-event-modeling-strategy]] (2026-08-21) and [[dilger-markdown-is-a-suggestion-dressed-as-a-spec]] (2026-08-25). **[[model-as-code-vs-model-as-language]] is now grounded** — its two central block quotes cite source pages instead of raw files, and the sourcing note that asked for this revisit is replaced by a new section on what each side's full material adds. Three things the quotes alone hid: (1) **Miller's argument is economic and track-record-based, not philosophical** — the authoring surface is expensive and JHipster/low-code disappointed before — which makes it falsifiable in principle and unfalsified in practice; (2) **Dilger does not reject code as a formal language** ("We have a formal language - it's called Code"); his objection is that code specifies implementation while the thing needing specification is behavior over time, and his economy-of-description test targets *prose*, where the two men fully agree; (3) Miller's **direction of fit is explicit** — "have the specified model overridden when real code is built in the system." Both are far more hedged than their quotes suggest ("spaghetti against the wall", "pretty mushy"; "the one that works *for me*"), so the page stays unresolved. Extended [[agent-readable-model-artifacts]] with the **derived rung**, deliberately marked *off-ladder* rather than slotted in — the ladder ranks how tractable an *authored* model becomes, and a derived export inverts the dependency, so ranking them on one axis would smuggle in the conclusion; added a four-row comparison of what each direction buys (drift-immunity and non-developer reviewability trade against each other; gradeability is untested on the derived side). Also updated [[vertical-slice-architecture]] (Miller's token-economics claim, independently reached from the other pole — and his own admission it is unproven, which matters because this page's token argument rests entirely on assertion from both camps), [[spec-driven-development]] (the two answers to "if prose fails, what replaces it" — and the observation that Ng/Dilger/Miller are three independent critiques of Markdown-as-spec on provenance/language/cost grounds respectively, which is stronger support than any alone), [[jeremy-miller]], [[critter-stack]] (CritterWatch 1.0, Bobcat + its MTP Supervisor, Fisher-backed agent orchestrator).

## [2026-08-31] research | SDD counter-evidence primaries hunt — filed: 3 raw sources, 1 blocked. Closes most of the wiki's **most load-bearing single-source dependency**: [[spec-driven-development]]'s counter-evidence has rested since 2026-08-16 on [[ng-spec-driven-development-is-waterfall-in-markdown]]'s secondhand summaries. CAPTURED (3): **(1) Colin Eberhardt (CTO, Scott Logic), "Putting Spec Kit Through Its Paces: Radical Idea or Reinvented Waterfall?" (2025-11-26)** → raw/articles/eberhardt-putting-spec-kit-through-its-paces.md. THE "~10x slower" trial, with the numbers Ng only paraphrased: Spec Kit produced **2,577 lines of markdown, 689 loc, 33m30 agent time and 3.5h review** for one feature, versus **1,000 loc, 8m agent time and 24m review** for his ordinary iterative approach — *and the SDD run shipped with a bug the iterative one didn't*. Also supplies the argument the wiki lacked: "**Code is law because it is a formal language you can reason about**… specifications in markdown lack this formality" — which is [[jeremy-miller]]'s position stated eight months earlier, and a direct answer to [[martin-dilger]]'s "we have a formal language - it's called Code" from the *opposite* conclusion. Note the author was never named in the KB before (recorded only as "Scott Logic's SpecKit trial"). **(2) François Zaninotto (Marmelab), "Spec-Driven Development: The Waterfall Strikes Back" (2025-11-12)** → raw/articles/zaninotto-spec-driven-development-waterfall-strikes-back.md. Seven named failure modes (context blindness, markdown madness, systematic bureaucracy, faux agile, **double code review**, false sense of security, diminishing returns on brownfield). His distinct objection is about **audience**: SDD claims to remove developers but needs a business analyst to review requirements and a developer to review design, "the same mistake as No Code tools". 225 points / 191 comments on HN. **(3) Birgitta Böckeler, "Understanding Spec-Driven-Development: Kiro, spec-kit, and Tessl" (2025-10-15)** → raw/articles/bockeler-understanding-sdd-kiro-speckit-tessl.md. **The origin of the spec-first / spec-anchored / spec-as-source taxonomy the KB already uses as working vocabulary** — and the biggest find of the three, because of a section nothing in the KB covers: the **MDD (model-driven development) parallel**. She argues spec-as-source risks inheriting "the downsides of both MDD and LLMs: inflexibility and non-determinism," and makes the point that MDD's *parseable* structure bought tool support for writing valid, complete, consistent specs — precisely what natural-language specs give up. That lands directly on [[model-as-code-vs-model-as-language]], where Dilger's whole case is that a validated, structurally-diffable model beats prose: Böckeler's MDD history is the strongest available *prior* for that position and the sharpest available warning against it, from one source. Retrieved via live Chrome after WebFetch refused the URL. **CHRONOLOGY CORRECTION worth carrying into the ingest:** all three predate Ng (2026-03) by 4–5 months, and each cites the one before it. Ng is a **downstream restatement of an Oct–Nov 2025 critique wave, not its origin** — the wiki currently frames him as "the first outside-in critique of SDD", which is wrong and should be fixed when these are compiled. **BLOCKED (1):** Gojko Adzic's "Spec Driven Development - revenge of Waterfall or BDD taken to new level?" (LinkedIn Pulse, 2025-09-29) — headless fetch returns an empty shell and the live-Chrome read was refused for lack of a linkedin.com content permission this session. Still the earliest of the four (three weeks after Spec Kit's launch) and the only one from the BDD tradition; [[gojko-adzic]] remains a stub with no primary. Re-try on a live sweep with LinkedIn permission granted. Per the Research workflow, no wiki/ pages were written — these are raw captures awaiting an ingest go-ahead.

The Supervisor's motivation was attached to the wrong feature. Lesson worth keeping: when compiling two sources into a controversy page, the pressure is toward making the poles mirror each other, and that is exactly where fabrication creeps in.

In every case a careful qualification was written on the page nearest the source and a **tidier** version was written on the page that needed the claim to carry an argument. Compiling one source into six pages means re-earning the caveat six times and it degrades each hop; the generalizable fix is to put the hedge in the sentence the concept pages will quote rather than in a caveats section they skip. All three fixed as visible "be careful with this inference" notes rather than silent edits. **FINDING 2 — the Ng primacy claim was wrong in 8 places** (index ×3, spec-driven-development ×4, alvis-ng, birgitta-bockeler, overview ×2), all corrected: yesterday's captures show Böckeler (2025-10-15), Zaninotto (2025-11-12) and Eberhardt (2025-11-26) each precede Ng by 4–5 months and each cites the one before, so he synthesises an **Oct–Nov 2025 wave** rather than originating it. Three factual errors fell out of the same comparison and are fixed: the Scott Logic trial was on a **hobby app, not a production codebase**; "2,500 lines of Markdown in the spec phase alone" was the cumulative whole-feature total (the specify step was 230 lines, the *plan* step was the bloated one at 2,067); and "Fowler/Böckeler" is **Böckeler solo** — an error inherited from Ng's own mislabelling. ## [2026-08-31] research | Gojko Adzic — the last SDD primary, and a misattribution it exposes — filed: 1 raw source. Retried via live Chrome after this morning's LinkedIn refusal. `raw/articles/adzic-spec-driven-development-revenge-of-waterfall-or-bdd.md` (LinkedIn Pulse, **2025-09-29**) closes the four-primary set that `overview.md` opened on 2026-08-16, and it is the **earliest of the four** — three weeks after Spec Kit's launch, ahead of Böckeler (10-15), Zaninotto (11-12), Eberhardt (11-26) and five months ahead of Ng. **The capture immediately falsified the reason the KB wanted it.** The line quoted on six pages — SDD as "the revenge of waterfall or BDD taken to a new level" — is **the title of his post, posed as a question**, which the body answers *"It does not, really."* He never calls SDD waterfall anywhere in the text, and his verdict is warmer than any other critic's: "definitely something to keep an eye on… Teams looking for more structure in their AI code generation workflows might find it useful now." Ng deployed the headline as an "even the BDD pioneer says so" move and the KB amplified it for a fortnight. Corrected on [[spec-driven-development]], [[given-when-then]], [[alvis-ng]], [[gojko-adzic]], [[ng-spec-driven-development-is-waterfall-in-markdown]], [[overview]] and [[index]]. What he *does* argue is sharper for this wiki than what he was quoted saying: generated specs are **scope-of-work, not specification** ("this is not a spec, it lacks a ton of detail" — the real spec migrates into developer-readable tests, "a missed opportunity"), and Spec Kit lacks a **scoping phase** so it "went off the rails" until human-in-the-loop stopped being feasible. Note [[given-when-then]]'s collaborative-derivation reading of him was **the wiki's own inference**, now marked as such. Lesson: a rhetorical question in a title is not a position, and a secondhand quote with no link is worth what the link is worth.

## [2026-08-31] schema   | vendor-claim marker convention adopted in CLAUDE.md — Dannie's call after lint finding 4. **Interested claims now carry their marker at every use, not just on the source page**, because a caveat that lives only in a Caveats section does not survive being quoted. Four named categories: *vendor self-report* (a company's own numbers about its own product), *not independent* (shares an employer or commercial interest with the concept it corroborates), *preprint* (arXiv is not peer review), and *impression, not measurement* (do not promote a practitioner's "about ten times faster" to "datum" downstream). Applied to the known cases: Stripe's 1,000-PRs-a-week now marked as Stripe's own figure on all four pages that cite it; AHE downgraded from "peer-review-grade primary" to "preprint… though a controlled study"; Bayer/PRINCE no longer called "independent corroboration" on [[agent-explainability]] — it is Thoughtworks-authored and Thoughtworks coined [[harness-engineering]] — with that page's caveat rewritten to name what actually survives the discount (the EU AI Act text and the non-vendor ESAA preprint). The rule explicitly does not mean discounting these sources: vendor material is often the only material, and the practitioner closest to a thing usually notices it first.

## [2026-08-31] ingest   | Batch G — the two Willison autonomy bounds — touched: 4 pages, and [[unattended-coding-agents]] substantially rewritten. New source pages [[willison-breaking-claude-code-auto-mode]] (Rehberger's auto-mode bypass) and [[willison-just-a-rumour-of-a-bug]] (Madhavapeddy on exploit probes within ~ten minutes of a patch discussion), both **secondhand** — Willison relaying others, with both underlying primaries still uncaptured. The page had said since June that "the maker≠checker verifier sub-agent is what makes unattended safe"; that no longer survives, and it now opens by saying so. It carries **three independent limits, none of which a verifier catches**: *exposure* — the guardrail permitted the malware and then **blocked the agent's own cleanup command**, so a safety mechanism that can prevent recovery is not a partial control but a new failure mode; *capability* — other people's agents, not yours, with rclone going from ~20 disclosures in ten years to 40+ in a month at a 75% hit rate, and CVE assignment stretching from 2–3 days to 3–4 weeks; *degradation* — the [[token-budget-quality-cliff]], weakest evidentially and flagged as such. What they share is that each defeats a different comfortable assumption ("the guardrail will catch it", "we control the timeline", "the runs are alike"), so the question is not whether the agent is good enough to run unattended but **what happens on the run where it isn't**. Two details worth keeping: Madhavapeddy completed the task by switching models when one refused, so **vendor refusal is not a control**; and the auto-mode failure inverts [[autonomy-ladder]]'s reversibility principle exactly — the irreversible act was permitted, the reversible one blocked.

Settles the `[[adr]]` link that had dangled from [[decision-trace]] since the 08-30 lint, which had noted it "was deliberately NOT written rather than fabricated." Three things worth having: the format's scope question is **dodged on purpose** ("Do not take the term 'architecture' too seriously"), and the naming history records the ambivalence — renamed to "Markdown *Any* Decision Records" in 2022 and back to "Architectural" in 2024; MADR's **"Confirmation"** section ("how compliance with the ADR can/will be confirmed… a test with a library such as ArchUnit") is an acceptance criterion in all but name, and the fact that it is *optional* there and mandatory on a [[slice]] is a real difference between the traditions; and [[eberhardt-putting-spec-kit-through-its-paces|Eberhardt]] uses ADRs as his own counter-example while rejecting SDD — "Code can give you the 'what', but cannot tell you the 'why'." The KB holds no evidence that ADRs work; the open question is agent-facing, since an ADR corpus is exactly the structured "why" [[context-engineering]] wants.

## [2026-09-02] ingest   | Nick Tune + Rachel Laycock — the enforcement and governance rungs — touched: 10 pages. Dannie's call ("ingest"), taking the two un-ingested pieces that had no wiki presence at all between them. New source pages [[nick-tune-enforced-application-architecture-agents-humans]] (2026-08-13) and [[laycock-citizens-build-agents-execute-experts-govern]] (2026-08-19); new entity [[rachel-laycock]]. **The finding that changes a page rather than adding to it: [[model-as-code-vs-model-as-language]]'s two-column table was a false binary, and Tune occupies the missing cell.** His authoritative artifact is neither a model file authored before code nor the code's content, but the **constraint the code must satisfy to build** — *code ⊨ constraint*, enforced in CI, with nothing generated in either direction. Three details make it a distinct position rather than a variant: (1) **Markdown's failure is his premise, not his thesis** — the post opens with "AI-generated code does not follow architectural guidelines that are written in skill files, ADRs, and various other places in the repo" and spends its length on the mechanism, and note his target explicitly includes **ADRs**, which Miller never discusses and Dilger attacks by argument; (2) it answers Miller's *economic* objection without conceding Dilger's artifact, because a DSL describing permitted structure is far smaller than a model of the system and it pays out on every agent run rather than once at codegen; (3) **it cannot specify behaviour** — a role constraint can forbid an aggregate inside a value object but says nothing about what happens when a reservation is priced, so Tune's rules and an event model are complements, not rivals. Recorded on the page as a third grader alongside Dilger's structural diff and Miller's derived visualisation — and the only one of the three already running on someone's repo, as well as the only one producing no artifact a business stakeholder could read. **[[fitness-functions]] gained the section it was waiting for**: Böckeler's field-report caution that such rules "can only express what imports, file names, and folder structure allow" is the exact limit Tune sets out to move, with role-based rules (filter on layer + role: `/adapters` may import from `/domain` but only `domain-port`; `/data-access` may take `aggregate` and `value-object` but never `domain-service`) and role *shape* constraints (private constructor, branded member, `parse`-prefixed statics). The theory of enforcement is different from the page's existing one and worth the distinction: not "catch drift early" but make the wrong thing **structurally unimplementable** — "AI cannot implement an aggregate inside value-object because an aggregate-repository must return an aggregate. So it wouldn't be able to load it." Böckeler's deeper point survives: this is still structural, not semantic. **[[vertical-slice-architecture]]** gets its first source making slices *compulsory* (`/features/{feature}`, cross-feature imports forbidden outright) — which supplies the mechanism under the collision-avoidance claim the page has been asserting from the *intent* of slicing, plus an asymmetry the KB had not articulated: he leaves the domain model free-form ("domain models should express the business however necessary") while forcing slices to be uniform ("I do want things to be highly standardised and boring"), and the reason — comparability across slices — is also what makes Dilger's Slice-Cycle-Time estimation work. **[[adr]]**: the page's own open question ("do agents read them?") now has a data point and it is unflattering — Tune names ADRs among the artifacts agents ignore and moves the decision's *force* into the build while keeping the ADR for humans, "ADR-002 describes the same architecture for humans. The ADR and executable Rivière configuration are kept aligned." That is the KB's first instance of MADR's optional **Confirmation** section actually wired up, and the alignment is **manual** — two artifacts, one architecture, nothing enforcing the alignment itself, a drift risk neither MADR nor the post addresses. One practitioner's report is not a study; what he offers is a worked alternative. **[[business-capabilities]]** (the flagged-important thread) gained the two rungs it was missing and now tabulates **four**: strategy (Homann/Wardley, enforced by nothing — it is a map), organisation (Laycock/Skelton, enforced by people), data-and-agent-contract (Sadalage-Chandrasekaran/CEAD, enforced by the ACC), code (Fritzsche/Tune, enforced by the build). Fritzsche gives a capability a *home*; Tune makes the home's walls load-bearing. **The gap the table makes visible is now the page's best open question: nothing connects the rungs** — a capability on a Wardley map, a capability an ACC governs, and a capability that is a build-protected folder are three artifacts with no traceability, and no source here attempts the mapping. **Laycock** supplies the organisational rung without ever using the word "capability": "citizens build, agents execute, experts govern" — which she explicitly says is **not about roles** but about where value moves — plus the scarcity inversion this KB should carry ("what feels scarce now is good engineering judgement… knowing when something that works is actually safe to trust in production") and the expert's output being the *environment* rather than the review gate. On [[agent-governance]] that lands as the same inversion CEAD found from the design side, restated as a job description, which is a real convergence because **CEAD is outside and she is not**. **Both new pages carry interested-party markers at every use, per the 08-31 convention**: Rivière is Tune's own tool, the post is a series instalment ending "get in touch," and it carries no before/after numbers — mechanism described precisely, payoff asserted, and he splits his own verdict (package/domain/layer rules indispensable for agentic work, the role tier "come back in 6 months"). Laycock is Thoughtworks' CTO on martinfowler.com evidenced by a Thoughtworks event and conversations with Thoughtworks engineers — **not independent**, and framing rather than measurement (self-described ramble, no data, one anonymised team). [[thoughtworks]]'s concentration-problem section gains her as a fourth instance. **Also filed for later:** her second capture `raw/articles/laycock-the-conductor-developer.md` (2026-07-31) is still un-ingested and is the obvious companion — the individual-role view beside the organisational one; and the Laycock page flags `raw/articles/addyosmani-human-judgment-relocates.md` as the nearest second voice on the judgement-scarcity claim, so the pairing is not lost when Batch F runs. Pages touched: 2 new sources, 1 new entity, [[nick-tune]], [[thoughtworks]], [[model-as-code-vs-model-as-language]], [[fitness-functions]], [[adr]], [[agent-governance]], [[vertical-slice-architecture]], [[business-capabilities]], plus `index.md`. Backlog 32 → 30 (offset by today's 14 captures: net 44).

## [2026-09-02] verify   | fidelity pass over the Tune/Laycock ingest — 14 findings, 13 fixed, and the worst one is a new failure mode. A subagent re-read both raw captures against all eleven pages compiled from them. **Structure was clean** (307 pages carry `sources:` and every reference resolves; 161/161 source pages have `raw_file:` with all 166 paths live; index in sync at 306/306; zero true orphans; 27 dangling wikilinks, all deliberate forward references, no typos). **The fidelity layer is where the errors were, and finding 1 is a kind I have not made before: a quotation lifted from one source, reworded, and attributed to another.** [[vertical-slice-architecture]] had Tune's slice rule justified "per Tune, agents need it precisely because they *'copy what they find'*" — that phrase is **Jeremy Bogard's**, from `raw/articles/bogard-vertical-slice-architecture-webinar-recording-whats-next.md` captured *the same morning* ("An agent doesn't read your architecture diagram. It reads your repo and copies what it finds"), reworded, and put in Tune's mouth; the word "copy" appears nowhere in the Tune raw. This is the caveat-degradation failure mutating into an **attribution**-degradation failure: two captures arrive together, the sharper line gets attached to the page being written. Fixed by attributing the line to Bogard, citing his raw path, and stating plainly that Tune does not put it in those terms. The same phrase family had leaked into the Tune source page as a bare quoted "blast radius stops at the slice boundary" — also Bogard's, also unquoted now. **Finding 2, the second serious one: [[business-capabilities]] asserted the exact reading Laycock disowns.** She writes "At first I thought I was talking about roles… But I don't actually think that's what I meant. I think I was talking about where value is moving" — and the page called her slogan "a statement about which capabilities an organisation grants to whom", i.e. roles, as her claim. Three other pages carried her correction faithfully, which made the capability page the outlier; now recast, with the permission/accountability reading explicitly labelled **the wiki's extension, not hers**. **Finding 6 is the instructive one about markers:** the non-independence marker on two pages rested on "FOSE is a Thoughtworks event" — which the capture never says (it links martinfowler.com's *FutureOfSoftwareDevelopment* page and does not expand the acronym). The marker was right, its stated evidence was invented; re-grounded on the line the source does carry ("senior engineers at Thoughtworks"). A caveat can be correct and still be unsourced, and that is worth watching for. **Other fixes:** Tune's opening sentence had been silently rewritten into a flat declarative inside quote marks on three pages (restored in full); "already running in production" (the word "production" is absent from the raw) narrowed to "running on its author's own repo"; [[adr]] was the one downstream page using a Tune claim with no interested-party marker (added), and its "one data point… demonstrably ignore" softened to "one practitioner's report"; the "kept aligned **by hand**" gloss on two pages is compiler inference from a passive sentence with no agent — replaced with the honest form, *the post does not say what keeps them aligned*, which supports the same open question; the "structurally unimplementable" theory on [[fitness-functions]] was a generalisation from Tune's single aggregate/value-object case and is now attributed to the wiki, with his own stated mechanism (build failure) restored as the primary one; Laycock's source page called the FOSE story "an independent description" twenty lines under its own not-independent marker (fixed); slogan punctuation restored on four pages, since the three-sentence form is the point of it. **Finding 12 is a bias worth naming: five pages had tidied Tune's hedge in the *cautious* direction.** Every one quoted "still playing around with that. Come back in 6 months" and stopped — dropping his next sentence, "I feel confident it's the right approach." The 08-31 lesson was that compiling one source into six pages means re-earning the caveat six times and the tidier version wins; this is the mirror image — the more *sceptical* version won, five times, because scepticism reads as rigour. Restored on the three pages where the verdict is load-bearing. **NOT fixed, reported for Dannie:** (a) a **pre-existing slug collision** — `event-modeling-event-sourcing-podcast` exists as both an entity and a source page, so every wikilink to it is ambiguous and the index has one entry for two pages (suggested fix: rename the source page to `…-podcast-show-notes`, matching its raw file); (b) [[adr]] and [[spec-driven-development]] both link `[[eberhardt-putting-spec-kit-through-its-paces]]`, which has no source page because the capture is still in the backlog — and `adr.md` **quotes it verbatim** (quote verified accurate against the raw), so a factual claim on a concept page currently traces to no source page; ingesting the SDD quartet closes it; (c) `[[ubiquitous-language]]` is now linked from two real pages, not just the log, so it has graduated from forward reference to genuine missing page; (d) two soft orphans reachable only from `index.md`/`log.md` (two Dilger source pages). **One raw correction, mine:** the AxonIQ capture had the vendor judgment written into its `publication:` field — an argument about the source's motive, embedded in a file `raw/` treats as ground truth. Reduced to `publication: Axoniq blog`; the vendor flag belongs on the future source page and on this log line, not in the capture.

The `channels[]` lists in watch-config assume direct polling and no longer work as written headlessly. **KNOWN BACKLOG SURFACED, not captured (out of window):** Sara Pellegrini's newest post is still "The DCB Tag Dilemma" (2026-03) — outside even her 120-day override, still absent from `raw/`, and she is the DCB originator; Addy Osmani's "The Code Agent Orchestra" (2026-03-26) is un-captured and directly on-thread (Agent Teams mechanics, the Ralph Loop, "the bottleneck is no longer generation, it's verification"); martinfowler.com's "TDD inside the agent loop — theater or actual value?" and Latent.Space's "Software Factories" and "Flue 2" are all linked-from-captures but absent. None are new *this week*, so none were filed — but they are on-thread primaries the recency-bounded sweep structurally cannot reach.

## [2026-09-04] research | EM×agents + EM method-wide + substrate + loop-eng + people watch (scheduled run; web + named-channel axes only, social SKIPPED — headless) — filed: 9 raw sources. **Real new-material window was ~2 days** (the 09-03 run swept through 09-03), so the yield is thin by construction, and only 5 of the 9 are on a wiki thread. FILED (9). **Loop engineering / agentic-coding practice (5):** `swyx-gpt6-astra-automated-ai-engineer` (Latent.Space, 09-03) — swyx claims Astra-class models are "fully capable AI Engineers in their own right", running fleets of subagents with bounded concurrency and holding coherence over billions of tokens in one thread; **impression, not measurement**, and it carries an access-dependency conflict he states himself (OpenAI granted early access and "was most generous with trial limits so this gets the writeup"), plus it is an **unfinished draft** ("We are out of time for this writeup") — noted in the capture's `capture_note`. `willison-gpt6-astra` (09-03) is the useful counterweight and the one number worth keeping from the whole launch: **99.9% on ARC-AGI-3 with OpenAI's custom "Provider Adapter harness" ($19K) vs 62.7% on the default harness ($26K)** — a harness-not-model result, and **OpenAI's own self-reported benchmark**. `willison-introducing-wrapture` (08-31) — Graham Dumpleton's first fully agent-written project, with an unusually careful process disclaimer ("not vibe coding… the AI was the means not the source of the design"); a **self-report about his own process**. `willison-claudes-new-system-prompt` (09-02) — 2,270-word diff of the published Claude Fable 5.1 system prompt; the structural finding is on-thread even though the subject isn't: the feature/tool-specific prompt blocks are **not** published, so the released "system prompt" is one layer of the real harness, and note that claim comes from the model describing its own context, a **model self-report, not documentation**. `tornhill-beyond-lambdas-raising-the-abstraction-level` (Substack, 09-01) — lambdas optimise for writing at the expense of reading; name the lambdas, then name the pipeline steps, so the code reads as a story of the domain, tied back to his CLEAR principles and to minimising "reconstruction work" for humans *and* agents. **Practitioner judgement, not measurement**, and his own publication — but no CodeScene product claims in this one. **Verification burden (1):** `laycock-maybe-we-shouldnt-be-reviewing-all-this-code` (martinfowler.com/rachels-ramblings, 09-02) — the direct successor to her conductor/governance pieces and a real position rather than a restatement: do **not** automate review to keep pace with agent output; move what review is actually for (knowledge transfer, architectural alignment, collective ownership) *earlier* into pairing, team design sessions and fitness functions, and review by exception. Closes "We need engineers to understand systems, not diffs." **NOT INDEPENDENT** (Thoughtworks CTO on the Thoughtworks-hosted site — the same house that coined harness engineering), and the two figures she relays (Meta: LOC per human-landed diff +106% in a year; median PR size +64%) reach her **via Houck at DX, a vendor selling developer-productivity measurement** — secondhand vendor numbers; her "if an agent can produce ten times the code" is rhetorical, not a measurement. This pairs directly against [[willison-brewster-cannot-review-180000-lines]] (09-02): same week, same problem, opposite conclusions about whether review scales. **Event Modeling axis (1, and it is a missed-channel discovery more than a new item):** `dilger-podcast-episode-47-agentic-modeling-audit-trails` — Episode 47 show notes, the **newest podcast item anywhere** and absent from both the RSS feed and podcast.eventmodeling.org (which still end at Ep 46, 2026-04-27). Two agents (Claude Code + a Hermes agent) modeling alongside Dilger on a live model, plus a "what do you think" skill that points an agent at a model to post clarifying-question comments on slices — and the tuning lesson that restricting it to comment only on what is *present*, rather than inventing hypothetical gaps, cut ~100 noisy comments to useful ones. Dymitruk's counterpoint: agents need an **audit trail, not a snapshot**. **The page is undated** in HTML, metadata and body, so it is filed `published: unknown` with the dating signals recorded in a capture note (it post-dates 2026-04-27; announces Golo Roden for an "already sold-out" conference) — **treat the date as unresolved, and note it may be a channel prior sweeps never polled rather than a new-this-week item**. **Vendor self-report** throughout: eventmodelers.ai is Dilger's own commercial platform. **Two deliberate off-thread captures, flagged for Dannie's call** (permitted by `people_scope: anything-substantive`, both from tracked authors, both droppable): `willison-claude-fable-5-1-animated-pelican` (09-01) — a model review, kept only for the reasoning-effort cost cliff (`max` = 65,927 output tokens, 13m54s, $3.30 vs ~10c at low), which is a loop-budget datum; and `fowler-paracelsus-maxim` (09-02) — "the dose makes the poison" as a design heuristic, ask not only in what contexts but **in what doses**, with global data as the worked example.
